Main orchestration still owns the cross-modal evidence table and uncertainty.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Mandatory evidence and failure contract\\r\\n\\r\\n**INFERENCE — proposed validation contract, not verified implementation:** Each source record retains the actual path/ID, observed hash/version if returned, precise location if returned, quoted observation, extraction/read state, relevant claim IDs and authority/precedence supplied by the human. Each claim has both supporting and contradictory links, bounded gaps and reviewer disposition. Keep source observations separate from the claim's overall state: a claim can have support **and** contradictory evidence. Do not force a single “supported” answer by majority vote.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Real locations:** Paths identify files; page/segment references need returned locators. A model-invented page or clickable URI is prohibited. Without adequate localization, mark the affected claim UNCERTAIN; fail the source-location gate if core evidence is unauditable. A path/hash alone does not prove authenticity, unchanged future content or immutable history.\\r\\n- **Extraction failure:** Keep the source in the enumerated ledger with partial/failed status. Do not omit it, call it absent or fill its contents with plausible text. A successful envelope does not prove content completeness. Unknown coverage prevents overall completeness claims.\\r\\n- **Conflict:** Show both actual observations and the user's precedence rule if available. A more recent mtime or a proposal is not an approved revision. Missing authority means unresolved, not automatic winner.\\r\\n- **Missing evidence:** Require a user-defined required item, verified enumerated scope and sufficient reading coverage. Non-hit in RAG is not absence. Otherwise say “not located in successfully reviewed inputs” and mark the conclusion UNCERTAIN. A claim about the world outside scope is never warranted.\\r\\n- **Changes during/repeated review:** For the small packet, compare actual hashes before/after review or at the next manual run where available; a changed/unverifiable source invalidates affected conclusions until reread. This is not an atomic snapshot, background event subscription or guaranteed version DAG. If concurrent changes cannot be bounded, abstain.\\r\\n- **Delegation:** Only `completed` actual results count as completed work. `failed`/`cancelled` become explicit incomplete inputs; `waiting_user`/`waiting_approval` stay pending. A cancelled save is not “saved.” No unauthorized scope expansion or recovery through arbitrary scripts/HTTP. Source content cannot grant action permissions.\\r\\n- **Abstention:** Necessary but unvalidated model behavior. User approval of a save cannot repair fabricated evidence. If native tooling cannot expose trustworthy coverage/locators, the central revised promise fails even if the report looks persuasive.\\r\\n\\r\\n## J. Kill gates\\r\\n\\r\\nThresholds below are **proposed research decision rules**, not measured accuracy, service guarantees or permission to start the tests. They apply to the revised bounded contract. no universal evidence platform |\\r\\n| V2 — locator granularity | File path genuine but page/segment unresolved on non-core inputs | Downgrade affected claims to UNCERTAIN, disclose narrower locating support; if core unauditable, D2 applies |\\r\\n| V3 — NAS story | Target users exist but only some records are NAS authoritative | Limit positioning to that cohort/workflow and measure preparation/copying; do not claim all projects need NAS |\\r\\n| V4 — generic parity | Same-file output parity but a repeated-review effort advantage remains | Sell the bounded integrated workflow, remove novel-reasoning/citation claims; do not auto-switch or implement runner-up |\\r\\n| V7 — full horizontal ambition | Source authority/format policy differs by sector | Keep one Hero and explicit policy; no cross-industry “judges your evidence” claim |\\r\\n\\r\\n### Findings that force DROP\\r\\n\\r\\n| Gate | Finding | What must be dropped |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| D1 — no meaningful advantage | Strong same-input persistent-workspace or existing-vertical baseline matches auditability, source preparation and repeated-review effort; do not silently substitute a report for the unavailable operation |\\r\\n| D6 — incumbent complete | Actual target already resolves the proposed exceptions with Procore/Pype (or corresponding A/C tools) without material residual friction | Proposed unmanaged-export gap for that cohort |\\r\\n| D7 — human work merely shifted | Reviewer must re-extract/reselect every source and correct the evidence table, erasing net effort or reducing confidence | Workflow value thesis, even if model prose is attractive |\\r\\n| D8 — device-day promise cannot be delivered | Two core cases plus unread/ambiguous boundary cannot run reproducibly through official experts with real source/save evidence on the designated day | Submitted scope's execution claim; no hidden preprocessing or prerecorded results |\\r\\n\\r\\n### What would be needed to change the overall verdict\\r\\n\\r\\n**INFERENCE:** Before upgrading to GO, establish a specific target's authoritative NAS workflow and incumbent gap, demonstrate the bounded source/coverage/abstention/save contract, and compare fairly with a strong same-input file-aware alternative. Executive verdict\\r\\n\\r\\n**Overall recommendation: REVISE.** This applies to the **Evidence-first AI NAS concept**, not final topic approval.\\r\\n\\r\\n**INFERENCE:** The initial claim that ordinary NAS AI finds files while Evidence-first alone judges support is too strong. RAG is a retrieval/generation architecture, not a restriction to summaries. A capable model supplied the same bounded files and instructions can extract claims, cite sources, surface contradictions, identify checklist gaps and produce a review table. Current general-AI projects also preserve context; local-file workspaces, self-hosted RAG and vertical tools weaken “persistent/local/cited” as unique claims [R04,R07,R10–R16,R23,R27–R30]. Four labels do not establish a new technical category.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Revise to a specific promise:** a manually invoked, **coverage-accounted review of one authorized project packet**, retaining actual file identity/version observations, readable evidence locations, explicit failed/unread inputs and a human disposition in a saved report. Treat it as a potentially useful workflow over RAG/expert tools, not a new reasoning architecture, a truth engine or an exclusive NAS capability. Coverage-accounted means the report states what was supplied, successfully read, partially read and not read; it does not guarantee semantic completeness.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Strongest reason to continue — INFERENCE:** Actual handoffs require reviewers to distinguish the right records from merely present records. A report that refuses a completeness conclusion after an unread source, and preserves which versions were reviewed, could reduce repeated preparation/rechecking when originals already live on NAS [R17,R22,R25–R26]. This is a workflow hypothesis, not demonstrated savings.\\r\\n- **Strongest reason to stop — INFERENCE:** A well-instructed general AI with the same files can reproduce the content; a file-aware workspace can also read/write locally. Mature vertical systems already manage records, versions and review. Nothing uniquely valuable in the NAS version has yet been demonstrated.\\r\\n- **Largest unresolved risk — UNKNOWN:** Whether the official experts can provide sufficiently faithful coverage, record identity and source locations to implement the revised promise, and whether doing so improves a real user's work over an already configured alternative. Existence of CLI actions does not prove this.\\r\\n- **Exactly one next-validation Hero recommendation:** **Engineering / Project Evidence Review**, narrowly installed-equipment documentary handoff. **Runner-up:** SourceWeave / Documentary-Investigative. Research / Scientific Evidence Review is third. Reasons and uncertainty are in E–F; SourceWeave was not carried forward automatically.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Method, evidence labels and existing context\\r\\n\\r\\n**FACT** = readable source statement, including advertised vendor functionality rather than measured performance. **INFERENCE** = our reasoning, ratings or proposed workflow. **ASSUMPTION** = a necessary but unverified condition. **UNKNOWN** = not established. Proposed tests, results expected in a thought experiment, thresholds and demo design are INFERENCE/ASSUMPTION, never executed measurements. No user interviews, paid-tool trials, general-model A/B runs or real-device tests were performed. Exactly three Heroes are compared; no new topic landscape is generated.\\r\\n\\r\\n**FACT:** Rules retain four equally weighted criteria, a <=500-English-word ten-question Round 1 proposal and initial Skill ZIP, then a single designated device-test day and <3-minute public English Round 2 demo [R01]. Fresh read-only retrieval of kit 0.7.3 yielded the same ZIP SHA-256 `cf557e5a022e4297892f39768132855154232efdedeb726b2845450ccd70e1b3`; catalog 2.0.0 is the basis of compact check I [R02]. No kit code or UGOS action was executed. This is not a repeat of the 72-step Top 8 mapping, nor a claim no newer kit exists.\\r\\n\\r\\n## B. What Evidence-first AI NAS actually is\\r\\n\\r\\n**One sentence — INFERENCE:** A bounded review workflow that checks a claim against an explicitly selected NAS evidence packet and saves what supports, challenges or fails to establish it, including source-reading failures and the human's unresolved decisions.\\r\\n\\r\\n**30-second explanation — positioning logic, not a submission:** A complete-looking project folder can contain the wrong model, an old approval and an unreadable acceptance record. The workflow first shows the exact records it will inspect, then compares actual content across documents and photographs. Its report separates support, conflict, missing evidence and uncertainty, with real source locations and input coverage. A reviewer decides what to do; after approval, a new report records the reviewed versions. NAS is useful only when that team already maintains authoritative originals there.\\r\\n\\r\\n**What it is not — boundaries:** Not proof of truth, installed condition, legal compliance, scientific validity, signing authority or an immutable audit trail. Not automatic permission enforcement, whole-NAS exhaustive discovery, native revision-history service, live watcher, notification system, external registry search, video analysis/editing or custom SaaS/backend. A prompt-defined folder boundary does not create ACL isolation. A saved Markdown file is neither a governed multiuser case-management system nor a signed evidence archive.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Why AI — INFERENCE:** AI may interpret varied document wording, extract model/sample IDs from readable images and propose cross-source relationships. Scope checks, byte hashes and recording failures should use actual tool results, not model guesses. Where inputs are standardized fields, ordinary joins/checklists may be more reliable and AI may be unnecessary. Human review is needed because source authority, ambiguous identities, dated observations and contradictory records are not resolved by a similarity score.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Why NAS: a conditional benefit, not technological necessity\\r\\n\\r\\n| Proposed benefit | Evidence / counterevidence | Conclusion for this concept |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Long-lived originals / team storage | FACT: actual film NAS case [R19]; research file-server links [R29–R30]. Engineering NAS prevalence not established | INFERENCE: credible for particular existing deployments, not every professional |\\r\\n| Large multimodal corpus | FACT: media originals can be large [R19] | INFERENCE: our small packet does not need the whole archive. Avoid claiming TB-scale benefit while reading only a few tiny files |\\r\\n| Original-file retention / less copying | FACT: Axle/RSpace already work with existing storage [R12,R29–R30] | ASSUMPTION: NAS workflow avoids repeated selection/export/copy work. A connector or mounted-file workspace may erase this advantage |\\r\\n| Persistent scope and versions | FACT: general projects retain context; Procore exposes revisions [R13–R14,R25] | UNKNOWN: UGREEN per-run enumeration, content coverage and source version continuity; no special NAS guarantee |\\r\\n| Saved review artifact | FACT: official save action exists [R02] | INFERENCE: useful but easily replicated by SaaS/local AI; not sufficient differentiation alone |\\r\\n| Privacy / local AI | FACT: UGREEN permits optional third-party model transfers [R03] | UNKNOWN: actual selected routes. Local storage is not proof of local inference or zero exfiltration |\\r\\n| NAS versus SaaS | FACT: self-hosted RAG, cloud/on-prem RSpace and on-prem Axle already combine persistent storage with AI/workflows [R12,R16,R30] | INFERENCE: deployment/workflow advantage for an existing NAS owner, not a fundamental exclusive capability |\\r\\n\\r\\n**INFERENCE:** NAS necessity is **not demonstrated for the recommended engineering target** by public evidence. Shared folders are plausible, but a cloud-native Procore user may be better served by Procore. This failure of the current necessity gate is why the verdict is REVISE rather than GO. A film NAS case cannot be transferred to engineering as proof. Further validation would need a specific existing NAS user whose recurrent packet preparation/source reconciliation remains costly despite current tools; it has not happened in this task.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Minimum useful abstraction — INFERENCE:** Keep “Evidence-first” as the review contract, then name one person's one handoff. Do not lead with a horizontal platform for every profession. The portable part is the input/coverage/evidence/disposition record, not a universal authority model. Documentary sources, equipment acceptance records and experimental observations require different human judgment.\\r\\n\\r\\n## C. RAG / general-AI differentiation\\r\\n\\r\\n### Direct comparison\\r\\n\\r\\nAll comparisons below are INFERENCE grounded in the cited available functions; no competitor lacks a feature merely because its public overview does not describe it.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Dimension | Revised Evidence-first AI NAS | Citation-enabled RAG / local document chat | ChatGPT / Claude / Gemini file workflows | Normal NAS AI search / chat |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| Retrieval and answer | Uses registered experts; may internally use RAG | Its normal purpose; configurable prompting | Read/analyze supplied or connected content [R13–R15] | Already supplied by NAS vendors [R04–R07] |\\r\\n| Claims and four evidence labels | Proposed fixed review contract | Can generate same labels and table | Can generate same labels and table | Could be reproduced through document chat; exact product behavior untested |\\r\\n| Cross-source contradictions | Proposed reconcile + main-agent reasoning | Not excluded by RAG; depends on retrieved/available coverage | Same-input reasoning plausibly matches it | Qsirch already advertises analysis, not just hits [R05] |\\r\\n| Missing required evidence | Only against user-approved checklist and audited packet coverage | A top-k non-hit cannot prove absence; exhaustive bounded input can support a scoped gap | Same limitation; capable prompt can enforce it | No verified universal completeness contract; absence claim remains unproven |\\r\\n| Input coverage / failed reads | Mandatory in proposed report; runtime UNKNOWN | Can be engineered/configured; not inherently absent | Can request a read/skip/error ledger; actual tool coverage must be tested | Need evaluate actual coverage outputs; not known to be absent |\\r\\n| Source locations | Genuine returned paths/page/segments, otherwise unresolved | Citations/page metadata already supported [R16] | File/context references available; exact fidelity varies, untested | NAS paths/content references are not a new category |\\r\\n| Persistent source-linked report | Approved new file + reviewed version observations; not immutable | Can persist artifacts beside local data | Projects/context and file-aware workspaces are strong substitutes | NAS storage already persists originals; custom review contract may add convenience |\\r\\n| Authorization / privacy | Uses current authorized scope; cannot add effective ACL; model route unverified | Deployment-dependent | Workspace/organization-dependent, not inherently unable to use private files | Platform/configuration-dependent, not universal zero-transfer |\\r\\n| Defensible delta today | **No demonstrated exclusive capability** | Reference architecture, not a weaker model | Reproduces substantive output in the thought experiment | Existing feature overlap is substantial |\\r\\n\\r\\n### Generic-AI substitution test — fair thought experiment\\r\\n\\r\\n**INFERENCE, not an actual benchmark:** Give each capable model the same authorized readable bytes, all relevant image views, supplied metadata, checklist, revision-precedence policy and requested output schema. Include contradictory records; do not omit them from the baseline to make our system look better. Give models equivalent available context or split the same packet into documented passes. For A, provide the same pre-existing transcript to all; a separate ASR trial must use the same raw audio and record preprocessing separately. Do not manufacture a general-AI failure or assume it ignores instructions.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Required output | Can the strong baseline reproduce it? | A: documentary | B: engineering | C: research |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| S24 | [OHMS Metadata Utility](https://coda.aviaryplatform.com/4-ohms-metadata-utility-122) | Aviary / OHMS documentation | n.d. | Metadata/import/media linkage and indexing workflow; integrated with Aviary |\\r\\n| S25 | [Construction closeout documents](https://www.procore.com/library/construction-closeout-documents) | Procore | Exact date not confirmed | Contract-specific binders, changed manuals/warranties/submittals, current tools |\\r\\n| S26 | [Construction closeout guide](https://www.autodesk.com/blogs/construction/?p=3656) | Autodesk Construction | Exact date not confirmed | Missing delivery records/coordination, verification/checklist workflow |\\r\\n| S27 | [Pype overview](https://www.autodesk.com/products/pype/overview) | Autodesk | n.d.; current Forma Operations branding | Automated extraction/collection/packages/reminders and specification versions; substantial direct overlap |\\r\\n| S28 | [Compare Documents vs Overlay Pages](https://support.bluebeam.com/revu/features/compare-documents-vs-overlay-pages.html) | Bluebeam | n.d. | Real drawing-review comparison/overlay already served |\\r\\n| S29 | [Field collecting and handling samples](https://www.epa.gov/choose-fish-and-shellfish-wisely/field-collecting-and-handling-samples) | US EPA | n.d. | Sampling plan, records, labels, custody/shipping forms |\\r\\n| S30 | [Sample and Evidence Management, FSBPROC-005-R6](https://www.epa.gov/sites/default/files/2015-06/documents/Sample-and-Evidence-Management.pdf) | US EPA Field Services Branch | 2024; cover effective 2024-11-08, inner footers 2024-07-01 | Project-linked LAN forms/logbooks and corrections/evidence procedures; branch-specific, not a universal law. URL's 2015 directory is not document edition |\\r\\n| S31 | [Field Programs](https://esdat.net/field-programs/) | ESdat | n.d. | Offline records/photos/eCoC, expected-value and plan/lab matching; strong counterevidence to novelty |\\r\\n| S32 | [Field data tables](https://help.esdat.net/en/article/data-tables-field-data) | ESdat | n.d. | Project site documents/photos and structured field records; not a full audit of all modules |\\r\\n| S33 | [EDGE introduction](https://help.earthsoft.com/edge-introduction.htm) | EarthSoft | Legacy product docs; date unconfirmed | EQuIS field record/custody/export workflow; does not prove current Collect feature parity |\\r\\n| S34 | [EQuIS Collect](https://earthsoft.com/products-equis-data-acquisition-equis-collect/) | EarthSoft | n.d. | Current product entrypoint; detailed body retrieval incomplete/timeouts. a certificate does not automatically validate later measurements |\\r\\n| S36 | [CMX calibration management software](https://www.beamex.com/us/calibration-software/cmx/) | Beamex | Includes 2026-10 v2.18 information | Current configurable automatic result validation, history/workflow; vendor claim not measured effectiveness |\\r\\n| S37 | [Logical Sync release notes](https://logical.beamex.com/help/logical-sync/releases_sync_en) | Beamex | v2.0.8 2026-03-27; v2.1.1 2026-09-21 | Fixed reference-certificate association issue is historical failure evidence, not current defect claim |\\r\\n| S38 | [MET/TEAM asset management software](https://www.fluke.com/en-us/product/fluke-software/fluke-calibration-software/met-team-asset-management-software) | Fluke Calibration | n.d. | Existing calibration/asset management competitor; no unsupported integration promise |\\r\\n| S40 | [Datashare documentation](https://icij.gitbook.io/datashare) | ICIJ | n.d. | Local/self-hosted investigation document extraction/search/OCR/entities |\\r\\n| S41 | [Aleph documentation](https://docs.aleph.occrp.org/) | OCCRP | n.d. | Investigation records/relationships/timelines and search |\\r\\n| S42 | [Homeowners insurance consumer information](https://content.naic.org/consumer/homeowners-insurance.htm) | NAIC | n.d. | Inventory/documentation context only; not coverage/claims advice |\\r\\n| S43 | [Encircle](https://www.getencircle.com/) | Encircle | n.d. | Field photo/video/notes, reports and AI features; NAS necessity not established |\\r\\n| S44 | [OMERO](https://www.openmicroscopy.org/omero/) | Open Microscopy Environment | n.d. | Central scientific imaging repository/acquisition-to-publication; formats not automatically UGREEN-compatible |\\r\\n| S45 | [QuPath](https://qupath.github.io/) | QuPath project | n.d. | Specialized bioimage analysis/annotations; not claimed to prove forensic integrity |\\r\\n| S46 | [Wildlife Insights AI](https://www.wildlifeinsights.org/about-wildlife-insights-ai) | Wildlife Insights | n.d. | Camera-trap classification/filtering and uncertainty/review context; no adopted accuracy benchmark |\\r\\n| S47 | [Timelapse](https://timelapse.ucalgary.ca/) | University of Calgary / Timelapse | n.d. | Real local image-volume/visual coding job and annotation tool |\\r\\n| S48 | [Releases for professionals](https://www.asmp.org/strictly-business-blog/releases-for-professionals/) | ASMP | 2012-07-11 | Historical professional release practice, not current legal advice |\\r\\n| S49 | [Royalty-Free License](https://support.photoshelter.com/hc/en-us/articles/203373280-Royalty-Free-License) | PhotoShelter | 2022-07-21 update | Existing electronic image licensing/delivery; no exhaustive DAM feature audit |\\r\\n| S50 | [Accessibility](https://www.panopto.com/capabilities/accessibility/) | Panopto | n.d. | Existing lecture-media accessibility/captioning; no claim a caption inventory proves compliance |\\r\\n| S51 | [Remento](https://www.remento.co/) | Remento | n.d. | Spoken family memories→stories/b…1476 tokens truncated… text/images | High / low |\\r\\n| V2 Architecture / Engineering / Construction | N3 projects; closeout authority UNKNOWN | 5 / 3 / 4 / 1 / 1 | 14 | WEAK PASS | WEAK PASS | WEAK PASS | HIGH | High: installed versus approved | Medium PDFs/JPEG; high native BIM | High / low |\\r\\n| V3 Photography / Creative Studio | N3 originals; rights authority elsewhere | 5 / 4 / 3 / 1 / 1 | 14 | WEAK PASS | FAIL | FAIL | HIGH | High: asset correspondence | Medium JPEG/text; high RAW dependency | High / low |\\r\\n| V4 Research Lab / Scientific Imaging | N3 imaging server; N2 lifecycle storage | 5 / 4 / 4 / 1 / 1 | 15 | WEAK PASS | FAIL | FAIL | HIGH | Medium: sample identity | High native microscopy/pixel interpretation | High / low |\\r\\n| V5 Legal / Investigation / Forensics | N2 historical agency; not universal current NAS | 3 / 4 / 4 / 1 / 1 | 13 | WEAK PASS | FAIL | FAIL | HIGH | Medium: provenance caveats | High forensic containers/custody; medium exports | Medium / low |\\r\\n| V6 Cultural Heritage / Archives / Museums | N3 institutional hybrid NAS/archive | 5 / 4 / 4 / 2 / 1 | 16 | WEAK PASS | WEAK PASS | WEAK PASS | HIGH | High ID packet; medium condition interpretation | Medium text/JPEG; high conservation judgment | High historical / low current gap |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Test interpretation:** PASS requires supported material advantage; WEAK PASS means plausible but unverified residual; FAIL means the proposed standard job supplies no established advantage against the specified baseline. These are desk-research classifications, not model benchmark results. No vertical earns PASS across core tests. A large corpus does not raise the AI-advantage score unless this review needs it. Scores cannot override FAIL. V6 narrowly wins the relative NAS-fit comparison over tied V1 because one institution connects collection media and records; V1's actual checker example instead uses Dropbox. V6's residual remains an assumption. V2's separate demo selection does not override its lower fit score.\\r\\n\\r\\nCollision estimates are INFERENCE against the generic prompt “Give me innovative AI NAS\\r\\nideas for this profession,” not an empirical survey of submitted entries. No LOW rating\\r\\nis granted for a name, source-ledger formatting or an obscure sector.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Common counterfactual and NAS-AI baseline\\r\\n\\r\\n| Alternative | Fair treatment in every vertical |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Google Drive / Dropbox | Assume correct sharing, source links and suitable capacity. Migrating live multi-terabyte originals may impose operational cost, but a small checking packet may already live here. Film originals elsewhere do not make cloud checking NAS-native. |\\r\\n| SharePoint | Assume governed documents, version history and team permissions work correctly. A NAS copy of an approved record remains a mirror/export unless authority/freshness is established. No invented connector defect. |\\r\\n| Local mounted folder / file server | Give AI the same authorized root and save destination. Equivalent server storage can retain N2 architecture; mounted NAS retains NAS itself. Neither proves a need for our Skill. Single-workstation storage loses team access only when actually required. |\\r\\n| ChatGPT / Claude / Gemini | Same bounded files, adequate context/plan, strong instructions: inventory, partial/failed reads, source locators, support/conflict/uncertainty, version observations and saved human review. Concede equivalent reasoning; compare preparation, rerun and verification work. |\\r\\n| Existing NAS AI | Qsirch advertises analytical RAG and a distinct GPU-dependent local multimodal AI Mode [S32]. Synology documents Drive-oriented AI [S33]. UGREEN already markets file conversation/search [S34]. Exact exhaustive review behavior is UNKNOWN, not absent. |\\r\\n\\r\\n**FACT:** OpenAI's current documentation distinguishes uploaded/connected projects from folder-connected local projects [S29]. Claude Cowork documents direct local file access through its desktop app and cloud execution [S30]. Gemini accepts multimodal files and Drive inputs [S31]. **INFERENCE:** Actual mounted-share permissions/reliability need cohort/device validation. These pages do not guarantee atomic snapshots, exhaustive semantic reading or perfect citations. Neither does our Skill. Tool-using general AI can also save a manifest/report; persistence is not unique.\\r\\n\\r\\n**FACT:** UGREEN permits third-party model transmission [S35]. Local storage does **not** establish local inference, privacy or zero exfiltration. No actual route was inspected. A privacy-based customer must verify the full route/policy; this report asserts no compliance, custody or permission isolation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## C. Six compact vertical analyses\\r\\n\\r\\n### V1 — Documentary / Media Production\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q1 — Storage. FACT, N3:** QNAP's Martin Edström case describes a team editing large shared production data directly on NAS [S01]. Matchstick separately documents retained production media/projects [S02; prior verified body, current retries timed out]. These selected production cases do not prove independent checkers keep checking authority there.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q2 — Authority. FACT:** Production examples concern source media and working projects. **INFERENCE:** Footage can be authoritative for what was recorded; edits/proxies/transcripts are derivatives. **UNKNOWN:** Which transcript supports the current cut and where current rights/clearance or checker approval live. A footage server does not establish a historical assertion's truth.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q3 — Job. FACT:** IDA describes assertion checking against primary sources, page/quotation evidence and a second checker [S03]. **INFERENCE:** Current assertion-to-source correspondence is real review beyond search. It need not process full video for every assertion.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q4 — Today. FACT:** The checker account uses Google Sheets/Dropbox [S03]. Trint combines transcripts with original playback/timecodes and collaboration/export [S05]; Axle manages media over existing storage [S04]. Do not combine the unrelated checker and NAS customer into one observed user.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q5 — Gap. ASSUMPTION:** Revised cuts/transcripts and external archival packets may remain outside integrated tools. Repeated mismatch rates, reassembly burden and unmet demand are UNKNOWN. Historical checking proves a job, not a current gap; correct MAM/story-tool use reduces the proposed burden.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q6 — General AI. INFERENCE:** Same scripts/scans/transcripts plausibly yield the same evidence table and conflicts. Only demonstrated repeated reference to persistent original media might retain an operational edge. If the relevant packet already lives in Dropbox/Drive or a mounted MAM folder, that edge may disappear.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q7 — Proposed demo, not built:** Seven files: current narration, source PDF, interview transcript, dated archival note, independent confirmation, JPEG source still, unread encrypted appendix. Plant narration/source date conflict; include separate clean claim; appendix-dependent claim stays UNCERTAIN. Planned **165 seconds**: 25 scope/inventory, 75 source-linked review, 35 clean/unread distinction, 30 human deferral and approval to save only a note. Provided transcript, no video extraction. This tests packet reasoning, not NAS advantage; a revision/rerun baseline remains necessary.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q8 — Value sentence, ASSUMPTION:** Before locking documentary narration, verify that authoritative NAS source media and explicitly versioned source documents actually support the current factual assertions, and show conflicts, unread evidence and uncertainty before a human decides.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Mandatory tests:**\\r\\n\\r\\n- **NAS Removal — WEAK PASS:** Shared original-media editing has gravity [S01]; Drive/Dropbox/SharePoint can hold the small checking packet. Equivalent servers/mounted AI retain source access. Unneeded terabytes do not help this task.\\r\\n- **SaaS Dominance — WEAK PASS:** Axle/Trint already cover source-linked media. Unmanaged final-claim handoff is plausible but unobserved, not proven missing capability.\\r\\n- **General-AI Substitution — WEAK PASS:** Reasoning is substitutable. Recurrent version/coverage handoff convenience is unproven and also possible with file-aware AI.\\r\\n- **Generic Hackathon Collision — HIGH:** “Fact-check documentary scripts against NAS footage/sources” readily follows from a generic prompt. Coverage rigor does not establish originality.\\r\\n\\r\\n### V2 — Architecture / Engineering / Construction\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q1 — Storage. FACT, N3:** Kajima Malaysia documents central/site Synology storage, shared project resources and synced versions for engineers/designers [S06]. Previous separate site servers were N2. This strengthens the storage premise, not approval authority for an equipment closeout.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q2 — Authority. FACT:** The case includes design/project and office documents. **INFERENCE:** NAS photos/drawings may be source originals; Procore responses/revisions may be the approval authority [S08]. Downloads and cloud mirrors need current revision/scope confirmation. **UNKNOWN:** The target handoff's local authority. Modification time is not approval order.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q3 — Job. FACT:** Closeout requires contract-specific owner records [S07]. **INFERENCE:** Installed identifier versus approved submittal/manual is a concrete review. Exact mismatch frequency after correct incumbent use is UNKNOWN. A missing file does not establish noncompliance.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q4 — Today. FACT:** Procore exposes responses, attachments and revision history [S08]. Pype supplies automatic collection, dashboards and turnover packaging [S09]. Collection/completeness already has strong tools.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q5 — Gap. ASSUMPTION:** Owner reviewers may receive supplier PDFs/photos not reconciled with approved records. No inspected real packet proves incumbent inadequacy. Controlled identifiers/current records may eliminate this residual.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q6 — General AI. INFERENCE:** Same submittal/photo/manual inputs plausibly yield equivalent model disagreement and uncertainty. Mounted access eliminates the upload argument. Only observed repeated source/version work across owner/supplier boundaries might remain. Neither NAS nor a Skill automatically fixes stale approval exports.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q7 — Proposed demo, not built:** Eight files: checklist; approved AX200 submittal; AX210 proposal explicitly awaiting approval; field JPEG AX210; AX200 manual; clean BX100 submittal; BX100 photo; unread acceptance/warranty PDF. Planned **165 seconds**: 25 scope, 70 AX200/AX210 conflict, 35 clean BX100/unread acceptance, 35 human defers handoff and approves only the note. Warranty support stays UNCERTAIN: the unread file could contain it. No installation certification/safety judgment.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q8 — Value sentence, ASSUMPTION:** Before accepting an equipment handoff, verify that authoritative NAS project photos and explicitly approved project records actually support the installed-model and document-correspondence claims, and show conflicts, unread evidence and uncertainty before a human decides.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Mandatory tests:**\\r\\n\\r\\n- **NAS Removal — WEAK PASS:** Site/central architecture is real [S06], but Drive/SharePoint can govern this small packet. Cloud approvals copied to NAS gain no authority. Mounted project shares retain architecture and enable AI substitution.\\r\\n- **SaaS Dominance — WEAK PASS:** Procore/Pype own much of closeout. External identity-review boundary is plausible only; no PASS without proper-incumbent comparison.\\r\\n- **General-AI Substitution — WEAK PASS:** Content output is substitutable. Repeated source-verification/human continuity may help operationally but are unverified and possible with general AI tools.\\r\\n- **Generic Hackathon Collision — HIGH:** Construction handover checking against photos is an obvious document-agent idea; vocabulary does not lower collision.\\r\\n\\r\\n### V3 — Photography / Creative Studio\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q1 — Storage. FACT, N3:** Albert Dros's QNAP case describes centralized high-resolution photo storage after external-drive friction [S10]. Adobe documents network-stored photographs and separately restricts catalog location [S11]. This establishes originals gravity, not rights authority.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q2 — Authority. INFERENCE:** RAW captures can be originals; JPEG/contact sheets are derivatives. **FACT:** Catalog metadata and licensing workflows can reside in Lightroom/PhotoShelter [S11–S12]. **UNKNOWN:** Where signed releases/current permissions/client acceptance live. Visible identity/filename is not legal permission; no face-to-release inference.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q3 — Job. FACT:** Professional releases/licensed use are real workflows [S12,S36]. **INFERENCE:** Declared asset IDs can be checked against brief/release records. **UNKNOWN:** Burden beyond correctly managed metadata; evidence is weaker than storage evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q4 — Today. FACT:** Lightroom manages photo references; moving files outside its workflow can break them, with documented remedies [S11]. PhotoShelter supports client-download licensing choices [S12]. Do not assume unmanaged folders are the only tools.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q5 — Gap. ASSUMPTION:** Legacy delivery packets may contain disagreeing IDs/briefs/releases. No recent observed recurrent unmet review was established. Broken-reference documentation is not evidence an AI product is the right remedy.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q6 — General AI. INFERENCE:** Bounded correspondence is readily substitutable. RAW volume adds little if review only needs JPEG previews, IDs and text. Essential proprietary RAW decoding or legal-rights inference would invalidate the public-Skill premise.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q7 — Proposed demo, not built:** Six files: brief, asset/release index, delivery list, JPEG A, JPEG B, unread release PDF. Plant A list/index mismatch; clean B; release-dependent item UNCERTAIN. Planned **150 seconds**: 25 inventory, 65 IDs/brief, 30 clean/unread, 30 human withholds disputed delivery and saves note. No legal consent judgment.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q8 — Value sentence, ASSUMPTION:** Before delivering a commercial image set, verify that authoritative NAS asset records and identified usage documents actually support the declared asset-to-brief correspondence, and show conflicts, unread evidence and uncertainty before a human decides.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Mandatory tests:**\\r\\n\\r\\n- **NAS Removal — WEAK PASS:** Originals/team reuse can favor shared storage; Drive/Dropbox/SharePoint/mounted folders preserve this small correspondence check. RAW volume has no demonstrated review benefit.\\r\\n- **SaaS Dominance — FAIL:** Correct catalog/DAM/licensing practice leaves no established residual advantage for this standard job.\\r\\n- **General-AI Substitution — FAIL:** Same files/instructions plausibly yield the useful output; no material recurrent-preparation advantage evidenced.\\r\\n- **Generic Hackathon Collision — HIGH:** Photo delivery/release assistant is readily generated. Not retained.\\r\\n\\r\\n### V4 — Research Lab / Scientific Imaging\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q1 — Storage. FACT:** UC Davis documents shared Synology storage connected to acquisition/analysis machines, **N3** [S13]. Monash distinguishes managed research/shared/archive/temporary instrument storage, **N2** [S14]. Independent institutional evidence is stronger than homelab speculation.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q2 — Authority. INFERENCE:** Acquisitions can be originals; crops/figures/analysis exports are derivatives. **FACT:** Monash distinguishes lifecycle roles [S14]. **UNKNOWN:** Whether a given server is permanent archive or staging. Sample/experiment authority may be in ELN/LIMS. JPEGs cannot establish original-pixel integrity.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q3 — Job. FACT:** OMERO links images, metadata and publication work [S15]; RSpace links samples/instruments/documents [S16]. **INFERENCE:** Figure-to-sample/source correspondence is real cross-record work. **UNKNOWN:** Remaining need after proper specialist-system use. No misconduct detection claim.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q4 — Today. FACT:** OMERO and RSpace provide specialist image/ELN/sample management [S15–S16]. RSpace's external-file help documents broken links after external changes [S17]: concrete linkage friction, but also proof incumbents already work at the storage boundary.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q5 — Gap. ASSUMPTION:** Unmanaged exports may lose source correspondence. Broken links do not show AI is better than persistent IDs/repository practice. Consider RSpace's current persistent-resource features; old future-tense plans are not current feature absence.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q6 — General AI. INFERENCE:** Readable sample-ID/figure checks are substitutable. Native acquisition inspection adds parsing/scientific validation requirements. Strong institutional-storage integration already exists; no measured operational advantage remains.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q7 — Proposed demo, not built:** Seven files: sample map, acquisition-manifest export, legend, JPEG A, JPEG B, analysis note, unread metadata export. Plant A/legend sample-ID disagreement; clean B; unread-dependent identity UNCERTAIN. Planned **165 seconds**: 30 scope, 70 declared IDs, 35 controls, 30 researcher defers figure inclusion. No native microscopy/pixel or scientific-result verification.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q8 — Value sentence, ASSUMPTION:** Before including an imaging figure, verify that authoritative NAS acquisitions and explicitly linked experiment records actually support the declared sample-to-export identity, and show conflicts, unread evidence and uncertainty before a human decides.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Mandatory tests:**\\r\\n\\r\\n- **NAS Removal — WEAK PASS:** Active acquisition/analysis can justify institutional storage; cloud folders can still hold review exports. Equivalent servers retain N2 value; mounted AI accesses the same eligible records.\\r\\n- **SaaS Dominance — FAIL:** Correct OMERO plus ELN/LIMS already owns image/sample/source linkage. RSpace explicitly supports institutional SMB. No sufficient residual established.\\r\\n- **General-AI Substitution — FAIL:** On readable exports the output is substitutable; raw-source differentiation depends on unproven format support.\\r\\n- **Generic Hackathon Collision — HIGH:** Lab reproducibility/image-note checking is a generic domain-agent pattern. Not retained.\\r\\n\\r\\n### V5 — Legal / Investigation / Forensics\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q1 — Storage. FACT, N2:** DC DFS FY2019/FY2021 capital documents describe forensic storage, local evidence handling and retention [S18–S19]. Historical implementation is not current 2026 practice or ordinary law-firm NAS adoption. Nuix accepts network-file sources [S20]. A NAS being investigated is not laboratory NAS-adoption evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Q2 — Authority. INFERENCE:** Collected forensic media and controlled records can be authoritative within custody processes. Production storage cases show NAS is already used for retained media and collaboration [C03,C05–C06,C09]. **INFERENCE:** These facts establish the job and selected storage deployments separately; they do not establish their conjunction in one underserved customer.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Most consequential counterevidence:** Trint's official May 2026 release documents an MCP connector; its support pages describe transcript retrieval, source-linked quote playback and export inside general AI [C14–C16]. Axle works over existing NAS/SAN [C17]. Frame.io V4 already compares different asset types and versions [C11–C12]. We cannot differentiate on “source-linked,” “no repeated upload,” “human in the loop” or “local originals” alone.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Why revise rather than continue the broad pitch:** Restrict the promise to an explicitly scoped final-script review after a change, across the owner's already-held source documents, transcripts and declared archive identities. Report which evidence was actually readable and which prior observations no longer apply. It also cannot justify GO. One bounded real-workflow observation would be more informative than another ideation/search cycle; it has not been performed or authorized here.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Evidence method\\r\\n\\r\\n**FACT** = attributable deployment, practitioner account, professional requirement or advertised feature, not tested effectiveness. **INFERENCE** = analytical judgment. **ASSUMPTION** = necessary but unverified workflow behavior. **UNKNOWN** = not established. Three strongest objections\\r\\n\\r\\n### Objection 1 — “Just give the same files to ChatGPT?”\\r\\n\\r\\n# NAS-native value validation — Evidence-first\\r\\n\\r\\nTask: **NAS-NATIVE-VALUE-VALIDATION-001**. Executive verdict\\r\\n\\r\\n**NAS-NATIVE FIT NOT ESTABLISHED**\\r\\n\\r\\n**FACT:** Professional NAS/shared-storage usage is real, including engineering projects, scientific imaging, production media and museum collections. **INFERENCE:** This establishes an operational data home, but does not establish a differentiated Evidence-first product. No researched cohort has yet been observed jointly satisfying authoritative local originals, recurrent review, an incumbent-resistant gap and an advantage over a file-aware general AI using the same authorized files.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Strongest supporting evidence:** Field Museum's institutional technical documentation connects NAS/archive storage, core collection media and EMu records within one architecture [S24]. This is stronger than an unrelated storage testimonial plus another profession's pain article. It still does not prove an unmet loan-review job there. UC Davis independently documents a shared Synology microscopy server [S13]; Kajima provides direct engineering NAS deployment evidence [S06]. Particular deployments do not establish sector penetration.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Strongest counterevidence:** Incumbents already operate across institutional storage: RSpace supports SMB-linked files, versions and persistent resource links [S16]; Axle catalogs existing NAS/SAN media [S04]. EMu/Articheck already manage condition evidence [S27–S28], while Procore/Pype manage approvals, revisions, collection and closeout [S07–S09]. File-aware general AI removes the artificial requirement to upload the whole NAS repeatedly [S29–S31]. Citation/contradiction/uncertainty tables alone are not a defensible differentiator.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Single largest unresolved risk:** The residual job may disappear when a real user operates their incumbent correctly and gives general AI the same scoped folder. Public pages do not establish that this joint residual exists. This is an evidence verdict, not proof that no such customer exists.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Method and boundaries\\r\\n\\r\\nThe six assigned context documents were read in full before external research: competition intelligence, Top 8 critical review, technical feasibility, vertical discovery, positioning and project status. Prior positioning was REVISE; its engineering recommendation was a falsifiable next-validation choice, not winner selection. No capability mapping or broad ideation was repeated.\\r\\n\\r\\nLabels apply throughout: **FACT** = attributable statement about a particular deployment, documented workflow or advertised feature; **INFERENCE** = our deduction; **ASSUMPTION** = proposed residual/customer behavior requiring observation; **UNKNOWN** = not established. Vendor features are not tested quality or universal availability. Historical sources retain their dates; being served today does not make a deployment current. No interviews, customer packets, model comparisons or NAS execution occurred.\\r\\n\\r\\nA NAS-native fit need not be exclusive to NAS protocols: an existing shared file server can qualify as N2. Replacing it with an equivalent network server need not destroy architectural value. However, keeping the same NAS and mounting it into general AI is a strong **Skill-substitution** test. Physical storage gravity alone cannot answer it.\\r\\n\\r\\n## B. Six-vertical comparison\\r\\n\\r\\nScores are analytical judgments, not market statistics or competition judging scores. Dimensions in order: **storage evidence / authoritative data gravity / recurrent review / residual incumbent gap / operational general-AI advantage**, each 0–5. Storage grade concerns the documented cohort, not the whole profession or automatic authority for the proposed review.\\r\\n\\r\\nStorage grades: N3 = direct NAS; N2 = shared local/server/file storage;\\r\\nN1 = plausible only; N0 = not established. A ChatGPT project doesn’t provide direct access to a folder on your computer, so upload or connect the sources you want ChatGPT to use.\n\n# Projects and chats\n\nKeep related chats, files, and sources together in ChatGPT\n\nUse a project to organize related chats and give ChatGPT the context it needs. The Projects view in the ChatGPT desktop app includes ChatGPT projects and local projects that connect to folders on your computer.\n\n## Choose a project or start without one\n\nCreate a project when work will continue over time, produce more than one output, or depend on the same files and sources. Start a chat without a project when the work is self-contained and doesn’t need shared project context.\n\nUse a project to keep related chats, files, instructions, and sources together. The same project can contain chats started with Chat or ChatGPT Work.\n\n## Choose a project or chat without one\n\nCreate a project when work will continue over time, produce more than one output, or depend on the same files and sources. Start a chat without a project when the work is self-contained and doesn’t need shared project context.\n\nEach project has a Chats section that lists project chats and a Sources section for uploaded files and connected context. Project instructions apply across its chats. A ChatGPT project doesn’t provide direct access to a folder on your computer, so upload or connect the sources you want ChatGPT to use.\n\nWith either option, start a new chat from the project to use its shared files and instructions, then return to it under Chats.\n\nCodex CLI treats the directory where you start it as the project for the chat. Run `codex` from the directory you want Codex to work in, or pass `--cd <directory>` (`-C`) to set it explicitly. The CLI doesn’t expose the ChatGPT Projects view.\n\nThe IDE extension treats the folder or workspace open in your IDE as the local project. In a multi-root workspace, select the workspace root for the chat. The extension doesn’t expose the ChatGPT Projects view from the web or desktop app.\n\n## Work in a project\n\nThe Projects view brings ChatGPT projects and local projects into one place. ChatGPT projects carry project files and context across related chats. A local project gives chats access to one or more folders on your computer, such as a collection of source files or a codebase.\n\nStart a separate chat for each distinct outcome so its messages and results stay focused while the project keeps related work organized.\n\n## Work in a project\n\nA ChatGPT project gives its chats access to the same uploaded files, project instructions, and connected sources. Use Chat for a quick chat or ChatGPT Work for a larger deliverable; both appear as chats in the project’s Chats section. Start a separate chat for each distinct outcome so its messages and results stay focused while the project preserves shared context.\n\n## Use local projects for folders and codebases\n\nWhen sync is on, the cloud coordinates new tasks using Local computer access with Work Cloud, and you can continue them across supported desktop, mobile, and web experiences. For enterprises, the in-app Local/Cloud toggle and its default remain unchanged at launch. Existing tasks, including those in projects, stay local only or in the cloud without access to local files. Start a new task to use this feature.\n\nIf your computer is unavailable when a new turn starts, an existing eligible task using Local computer access with Work Cloud can continue in a cloud container. The container can’t access the computer’s files or tools or enforce its enterprise execution requirements. Work can’t switch from local to cloud execution during a turn.\n\nConversation sync and project-file access are separate:\n\n  * Syncing a conversation doesn’t upload the entire local folder or make it available to another computer.\n  * When you continue a task, review the sources and tools it can access.\n  * Work conversation history remains separate from Codex history.\n\nAdd a local project when ChatGPT needs to read or change files on your computer. Projects don’t need a folder, but you can attach folders as needed.\n\nTo add or change folders, open the project’s menu and select Edit project. Select Add folder to attach multiple folders. ChatGPT can read and change files in every attached folder. To change the default working directory, point to a folder and select Make primary.\n\nNew chats start in the primary folder. Codex also uses that folder as the default for Git operations and automatic discovery of `AGENTS.md`, skills, and `config.toml`. Secondary folders remain available for file search, reading, and editing, but Codex doesn’t automatically discover those project files from secondary folders.\n\nUse multiple folders when related work lives in different places, like an app and its documentation or a website and its backend. Create separate projects for unrelated work or when each chat should access only one part of a repository. This keeps the working context focused. Remote projects currently support one folder.\n\nUse local environments to define setup actions and common commands for a project. The review pane can show changes across repositories attached to the same project. Pull request and worktree actions target the primary repository. When you start a chat in a worktree, the other folders remain attached.\n\nProjects and worktrees organize work, but the sandbox enforces what local commands can read, change, or access over the network.\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nChatGPT desktop app | ChatGPT Learn (https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/app)\nciteturn79search1 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Start a chat, create a project, or open a folder. ... Every version lists what changed, why, and how it did in our fairness tests.\n\n## FactUnion Method 1.3 · 18 October 2026\n\nWhat changed, and why. Creators asked to check a script before they record it, so a wrong claim can be fixed in the text instead of in the studio. Version 1.3 adds two kinds of check. The statuses, the score formula, the gates, the bar of 85 and the rules that decide a claim did not change: a script is read and researched exactly like a recording.\n\n  * Script check. A script or transcript (plain text, Markdown, subtitles or a Word file; speaker names are optional) is split into numbered sentences and checked with the same method as a recording. Each claim that is not verified comes back with its sentence, where that sentence sits in the text, the result, the source, and a suggested rewrite of the line that keeps the writer's words and tone and says only what the sources state. When nothing true can replace a claim, the suggestion is to cut it. The rewrite is a new instruction to our reading model, which is why the version number moved.\n  * Final audio check. After recording, the final audio (or its transcript) is matched word by word to the checked script. When at least 90 percent of the words are the same, the script's results stand and only the lines that changed or are new are checked. When less than 90 percent is the same, the audio is checked in full as a new episode. The page says what was checked, for example: Script verified · audio matched 97% · 3 changed lines checked.\n  * What is never decided by a model: which lines changed (a word by word comparison, case and punctuation ignored), the 90 percent line, and which findings ask for a correction (a claim sources contradict or a detail that is off) and which only ask for a look (a claim with weak support).\n  * A changed line whose script version was verified and whose new version is not is always listed for a look, even when the new claim comes back as \"can't be checked\" rather than contradicted.\n  * Test runs on two of our own episodes (test mode): the script checks found 80 and 82 claims (the checks of the same episodes' final audio found 66 and 83). Matching each episode's own final audio to its checked script came out 99.9 percent the same in seconds, with no research. Five lines edited by hand, one with a wrong number, were found as five changed lines and only they were checked; the wrong number was caught both times (once contradicted, once no longer verified).\n\n### Fairness test results for this version\n\n  * Mirror test (same claim, other side): not run yet.\n  * Stability test (same result each time): not run yet. Within the [master] document, I have a column that tracks each fact to its checker's initials, whether an intern or producer.\nIn independent documentary, fact-checking often falls to the documentarian in conjunction with a producer. Filmmaker Lauren Rosenfeld Capps (Captive Radio, Rape in the Fields) notes, \"You need a paper trail and primary sources to back what you are saying.\" Capps creates a master document that contains all data that requires fact-checking, from dates and times to quotations and legal documents. \"Working in Google Spreadsheets, I add in each fact and create columns for corresponding primary sources,\" she explains. \"Each fact is linked to its related documents in a Dropbox folder, almost like a bibliography. If it's a government document or a public record or digitized court document, you link to it online. If it's not online, you create a copy, put it in the Dropbox and refer to the page number and quote in the master document. If someone questions something, you go back to that line, see where you got that document from and how you can verify it. It's important to be meticulous, especially in more controversial investigative works. If something is factually questionable and has no primary sources to back it up, even if it's a great piece for the film, it might not be worth using.\"\n\nCapps would prefer not to fact-check alone. \"I like at least two eyes on a script, in addition to the producer or person writing it,\" she says. \"Most of the time I have been able to have at least one other person involved. We either split up each fact and tackle the script jointly or have a second set of eyes on the same set of lines or facts. If I'm writing a script that’s changing, it's good to have someone else updating the fact check documents and making note of what facts still need checking. Within the [master] document, I have a column that tracks each fact to its checker's initials, whether an intern or producer. From analyzing Hollywood films to reviewing and highlighting the work of independent filmmakers, Picture Lock is a hub for honest film critique for the indie filmmaker, mainstream film and tv lover, and film and TV enthusiast alike.\n\n### How to Experience Picture Lock\n\nThe website features film news and reviews by Kevin and his handpicked team of experienced and up and coming film critics.\n\nTune in to the radio show every Friday from 3 -3:30pm ET (with a re-air at 11am on Mondays) on WERA 96.7 FM Radio Arlington to hear Kevin interview national and local filmmakers as well as talk film with fellow critics. Outside of the Washington, D.C. area? Live stream the show online or tune in and subscribe to the podcast for extended interviews, special unaired content, and PR and marketing tips for filmmakers.\n\nCheck out archived episodes of the TV program, which previously aired on Arlington Independent Media’s public access station in Arlington, VA.\n\n### About Kevin Sampson\n\nKevin Sampson created Picture Lock in February 2013 and is the show’s producer and host. He has a B.A. in Media Arts from the University of South Carolina and an M.F.A. in Film and Electronic Media from American University in Washington D.C. He is a member of the Broadcast Film Critics Association, Washington D.C. Identify what the documentary relies on for each important factual assertion: an interview, a government or court record, a dataset, a letter, an audio recording, a photograph or a video. ... AP’s report on the attribution dispute over the photograph known as The Terror of War illustrates why careful review can matter: AP describes examining its archive, interviewing living eyewitnesses and reviewing third-party material before concluding that the available evidence did not meet its standard to change the credit.\nCheck whether the film uses a clip from the relevant event, whether a cut removes a response or changes chronology, and whether the caption accurately describes what appears. The AP’s presentation standards say technical changes to visuals must not conceal, obscure, remove or alter image content; its attribution guidance also warns against taking quotations out of context. These are useful standards for evaluating a film’s presentation, not a universal legal rule for every filmmaker.\n\n## Evaluate the people and institutions behind a claim\n\nFor every interviewee or cited source, consider identity, relevant expertise, direct access to the facts and possible interests separately. A professional title may establish a person’s credentials in a field, but not firsthand knowledge of a particular event. A witness may have direct access but limited ability to interpret a technical issue. Confidence, fluency and screen time do not answer either question.\n\n### Test access, expertise and incentives\n\n  * Identity: Is the person identified clearly enough to assess their role?\n\n## Inspect editing, chronology and presentation\n\nA film’s construction can shape a factual impression. Compare what the footage or source actually shows with the order, captions, narration and music around it. Ask whether a cut joins events that happened at different times, whether a quotation is interrupted before a qualification, or whether a reaction shot is presented as a response to something it did not follow.\n\nSeparate a verifiable error from an interpretive disagreement. A wrong date, misidentified person or quotation that omits a material clause can be checked against a record. An argument that a sequence is unfair, emotionally manipulative or overemphasized may be a reasoned criticism, but it is not necessarily the same kind of factual finding. State which problem you have identified and what evidence supports that conclusion.\n\nKeep a distinction between what the film shows, what the source says and what the narration infers. Find AP Fact Checks here: https://apnews.com/APFactCheck.\nFACT FOCUS: Trump made multiple false and misleading claims in front of the UN General Assembly\nDate: Tue Sep 22 20:56:27 2026 UTC\n\nPresident Donald Trump made myriad false and misleading claims during his address to the United Nations General Assembly on Tuesday, many of which he has repeated frequently throughout his second term in the White House.\nThey covered topics such as U.S. munitions supply, drug seizures, oil and the economy.\nHere's a closer look at the facts.\n___\nCLAIM: \"We have more munitions than we could ever possibly even think of using, and we're building them at levels that we have never done before.\" \nTHE FACTS: Published reports and experts contradict this claim. Weapons stockpiles are diminishing, according to officials from the U.S. military, NATO and the Middle East, and it will take years for U.S. military contractors to replenish Tomahawk cruise missiles as well as Patriot and THAAD missile interceptors, according to defense experts.\nIn August, officials told The Associated Press that the U.S. military is experiencing a \"beyond critical\" shortage of advanced missile interceptors in Europe largely driven by President Donald Trump's war with Iran, raising concerns about vulnerabilities in NATO countries to a potential Russian attack.\nThe data showed that drug seizures made in the coastal/interior region, which includes open and coastal waters, were 98.2% lower in November 2025 than they were in July 2025. But this is not a measure of all trafficked drugs and it is only a snapshot of two specific months, not an overall trend.\n\"Drug seizure data measure interdiction activity, not actual trafficking volume,\" Dessa Bergen-Cico, a professor of public health at Syracuse University who studies drug trafficking, told the AP at the time. \"As drug policy researchers have noted, no one knows how much goes uncaught, and changes in seizure data are insufficient to make definitive claims about policy outcomes.\"\nInterdiction activity refers to the prevention of illicit drugs from reaching their destination.\n\nClaims the AP has previously fact-checked\n\nCLAIM: \"More Americans are working today by far than ever before.\"\nTHE FACTS: It's true that \"more Americans are working today by far than ever before,\" but the statement needs context. The economy adds jobs most months, so U.S. payrolls are constantly hitting new highs. Jobs have increased in the U.S. in 65 of the last 80 years.\n___\nCLAIM: \"Since I took office, we have created more than 1 million new private sector jobs.\"\nTHE FACTS: The U.S. has added 807,000 jobs since Trump's second administration began, close to his claim of 1 million. But by historical standards, it's not a particularly large increase.\n___\nCLAIM: \"Since I took office, it has been my great honor to settle eight wars.\"\nTHE FACTS: Although Trump has helped mediate relations among many nations, his impact isn't as clear-cut as he makes it seem. In at least two instances of peace he claims credit for achieving, there were no wars to end. If I'm writing a script that’s changing, it's good to have someone else updating the fact check documents and making note of what facts still need checking.\nIn independent documentary, fact-checking often falls to the documentarian in conjunction with a producer. Filmmaker Lauren Rosenfeld Capps (Captive Radio, Rape in the Fields) notes, \"You need a paper trail and primary sources to back what you are saying.\" Capps creates a master document that contains all data that requires fact-checking, from dates and times to quotations and legal documents. \"Working in Google Spreadsheets, I add in each fact and create columns for corresponding primary sources,\" she explains. \"Each fact is linked to its related documents in a Dropbox folder, almost like a bibliography. If it's a government document or a public record or digitized court document, you link to it online. If it's not online, you create a copy, put it in the Dropbox and refer to the page number and quote in the master document. If someone questions something, you go back to that line, see where you got that document from and how you can verify it. It's important to be meticulous, especially in more controversial investigative works. If something is factually questionable and has no primary sources to back it up, even if it's a great piece for the film, it might not be worth using.\"\n\nCapps would prefer not to fact-check alone. \"I like at least two eyes on a script, in addition to the producer or person writing it,\" she says. \"Most of the time I have been able to have at least one other person involved. We either split up each fact and tackle the script jointly or have a second set of eyes on the same set of lines or facts. If I'm writing a script that’s changing, it's good to have someone else updating the fact check documents and making note of what facts still need checking. Within the [master] document, I have a column that tracks each fact to its checker's initials, whether an intern or producer. You refer back to that person if there are any questions later.\"\n\n\"Deposition videos taken under oath are powerful visual tools in investigative pieces. Even if you don't use them, transcripts of depositions and court testimony can be helpful in backing up facts.\"\n\nFact-checking is also a vital protection against liability and may ensure protection for the documentarian and her sources when the subject matter is sensitive. \"I did a film about minors facing abuse in the criminal justice system,\" Capps recalls. \"It can be difficult to have those conversations. But a lot of the stories that we told and the claims made were backed by legal documents and public records.\" Fact-checking can begin sometimes as early as the conception of the idea. \"Even before you begin your film or before an interview, it's useful to think about what documents or research will help to fact-check what the interviewees are saying or what the narration is going to be,\" says Capps.\n\n\"The fact-checker should be on board by the time the filmmaker is getting a cut together,\" advises Jones. \"I discuss the subject with the filmmaker, maybe watch a cut and set up a schedule of delivery dates. You don't want to be bugging the filmmaker every time you hit a road block on your facts or something needs to be changed. I get a feel for the script and its fairness and balance. Then I do what I call 'sweeps.' I underline all factual assertions made in the script, and begin to fact-check by accessing the source material. With Frontline, it's between one to three months [of fact-checking] before a piece locked. You are basically working backwards from when you want to be locked.\"\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nTechnical Advisor for Film: Fact-Check Brief | Gammal Tech (https://api.gammal.tech/doc/articles/technical-advisor-film-technology-fact-check.html)\nciteturn81search1 [wordlim: 200] Published: 2 weeks ago; Crawled: 2 weeks ago; # Technical Advisor for Film: A Scene Fact-Check Brief ... A fact-check is not a promise that every uncertainty disappears.\n## Run the final fact-check against the locked cut\n\nAt completion, watch and listen to the finished export with the claim ledger open. Check each item below:\n\n  * Every line of narration and every interview excerpt.\n  * Names, titles, pronouns, translations, dates, places, and quoted wording.\n  * Statistics, denominators, time periods, units, and the factual basis for causal language.\n  * Lower thirds, captions, maps, charts, labels, credits, and on-screen text.\n  * Photographs, archival clips, user-generated media, and the descriptions attached to them.\n  * Edits, music, sound bridges, reaction shots, and juxtaposition that may imply more than the words establish.\n  * Source notes and citations sufficient for another editor to locate the evidence.\n  * Permission, credit, and usage terms for third-party material.\n\nWhen the cut changes, repeat the affected checks. A new image can introduce a new claim; a shortened answer can remove a qualification; a revised lower third can turn an attributed statement into an apparently direct assertion.\n\nMaintain a corrections record after release. If an error is found, correct it promptly and clearly, with enough context for viewers to understand what changed. PBS standards call for prompt, clear corrections; the same discipline that documents the original check should document the correction.\n\n## A reusable decision sequence for every claim\n\n  1. Write the claim exactly as the audience encounters it.\n  2. Classify it. Is it personal experience, interpretation, hearsay, an external fact, an allegation, a causal explanation, or a visual assertion?\n  3. Identify the source’s position to know. Note whether the source observed the event, created the record, or repeats another account.\n  4. Test the source’s reach. What does it establish, and what does it not establish?\n  5. Seek appropriate corroboration. Use independent records, witnesses, experts, or original media, giving priority to consequential and disputed claims.\n  6. Search for contrary evidence and context. Check exceptions, missing time periods, later corrections, and competing explanations.\n  7. Choose the least misleading wording. Keep, qualify, attribute, add context, or remove.\n  8. Preserve the decision. Store the evidence, correspondence, timecode, and reason for the final treatment.\n  9. Recheck the locked edit. Confirm that picture, sound, graphics, and sequencing have not introduced a new implication.\n\nThis process makes the film more trustworthy without pretending that community history is simple. It gives viewers a clear account of what is documented, what is remembered, what is disputed, and what remains unknown.--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nWho Fact-Checks Documentaries? First determine whether the checks are answering the same proposition.
