[{"type": "input_text", "text": "Script completed\nWall time 2.9 seconds\nOutput:\n"}, {"type": "input_text", "text": "newdocediting.com (https://newdocediting.com/stages-of-documentary-post-production/)\nciteturn85view0 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: yesterday; Content type: text/html; Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://newdocediting.com/stages-of-documentary-post-production/\",\"lineno\":null}); Total lines: 167\nL0: ### Stages of Documentary Post-Production\nL1: ####\nL2: \nL3: In the spirit of community-wide collaboration, we would like to share these time-tested post-production procedures, as well as some of our own editorial inventions, with other editors and directors.\nL4: \nL5: New Doc Editing first compiled this step-by-step post guide for its popular seminar “Editing the Character-Driven Documentary”, developed for the Film Arts Foundation and the San Francisco Film Society.\nL6: Remember, this guide is a suggested course of action; not a hard and fast prescription. Each project will likely deviate from time to time from these stages of post-production.\nL7: \nL8: We would like to thank all the documentary directors, producers, and editors who contributed to these professional guidelines.\nL9: \nL10: \nL11: \nL12: View Rushes and Logging\nL13: \nL14: Get transcripts made of chief interviews.\nL15: \nL16: Note scenes that move you, even little moments that strike you emotionally.\nL17: Note sound bites that make compelling points intellectually.\nL18: \nL19: Distinguish between scenes in which something actually happens, and scenes that will primarily function as b-roll over VO.\nL20: \nL21: In the scenes in which something actually happens, identify the actions that are relevant to the plot, i.e., to the protagonist’s quest.  If it’s not relevant, should it be in the film?\nL22: \nL23: What scenes/footage might work as inciting incident, act climax, backstory, turning points, film climax?\nL24: Update Doc Plot Map^{TM} (a customized plot tool we often use in our collaborations).\nL25: \nL26: \nL27: \nL28: Paper Edit\nL29: \nL30: When editing projects that are talking head heavy, editors often employ transcripts that are cut and pasted into a paper edit.\nL31: \nL32: The strength of the paper edit is that it can help organize ideas, an excellent tool for an essay film.\nL33: \nL34: The potential weakness of a paper edit is that your first edit will be dialogue heavy and you may miss the potential of verite scenes and visual moments.\nL35: \nL36: \nL37: \nL38: Assembly Edit\nL39: Construct an assembly edit after ninety percent of your footage is shot, digitized and logged and you have some kind of structure on paper.  This could be a paper edit, an index card outline, or a simple, preliminary timeline of your three-act structure.\nL40: The assembly cut should not be screened to anyone outside the film’s tight need editorial team.  It is a tool for editors, directors, and creative advisors.  Its function is to see the big picture, the film in broad strokes, when shooting is roughly ninety percent complete.  The chief questions that the assembly should answer are “Is there a story here?”  “Is there a film here?” “What’s the likely structure?” “Which characters and themes can be cut?”\nL41: For this reason, length should be no more than 140 percent of final film.  If longer, it is difficult to assess the film’s structure and rhythm.  For a 60-minute documentary, the assembly should be no more than 84 minutes.\nL42: \nL43: The assembly is your best first guess at structure.  If you don’t know where to start, try a strictly chronological approach.\nL44: \nL45: Include a little of every scene that might make it in the film.\nL46: \nL47: Include all your killer emotional moments.\nL48: Edit the assembly quickly, within a few weeks if possible.  Sequences should be bulky-represented by two or three long unedited shots.  Resist the temptation to finesse edits.\nL49: \nL50: Edit with sync sound.  No L cuts, J cuts or VO (voiceover).  This level of fine cutting can be a waste of time because you will may change scenes later or cut them entirely.  Also, you don’t need to see cutaways, etc. to tell is a film’s structure is working.\nL51: No narration, music, dissolves, cutaways, inserts, special effects. Jump cuts are fine.\nL52: \nL53: Use cards for missing interviews, archival footage, etc.\nL54: \nL55: After viewing the assembly, determine if a viable film exists. What additional shooting may be needed?\nL56: \nL57: What characters can be dropped because they are not pertinent or they are repetitious.\nL58: \nL59: What ideas/themes can be dropped?\nL60: \nL61: What scenes are not needed?\nL62: \nL63: Update Doc Plot Map^{TM}.\nL64: \nL65: \nL66: \nL67: Rough Cut\nL68: Your rough cut will be seen and evaluated by test audiences and funders.\nL69: \nL70: For this reason the length should be no more or less than ten percent of estimated final TRT.  This is so test viewers can accurately judge the film’s structure and rhythm.\nL71: \nL72: Lower distracting ambient sound.\nL73: \nL74: J and L cuts with voiceover are OK but don’t finesse edits too much.\nL75: \nL76: Include first draft narration as either on-screen text or scratch track narration.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nSaving files and version history (https://info.trint.com/knowledge/saving-files-and-version-history-trint-help-center)\nciteturn85view1 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: last week; Content type: text/html; Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://info.trint.com/knowledge/saving-files-and-version-history-trint-help-center\",\"lineno\":null}); Total lines: 68\nL0: 【0†iframe†www.googletagmanager.com】\nL1: \nL2: 【1†Skip to content】\nL3: \nL4: English Show submenu for translations\nL5: \nL6: 【2†Customer portal】\nL7: \nL8: Open main navigation Close main navigation\nL9: \nL10:   * English Show submenu for translations\nL11: \nL12:   * 【3†Customer portal】\nL13:   * 【4†Go to trint.com†trint.com】\nL14: \nL15: 【4†Go to trint.com†trint.com】\nL16: \nL17: Looking to do more with Trint? This guide will show you how to know when your file is saved and how to access older versions.\nL29: \nL30: This support guide will cover:\nL31: \nL32:   * 【5†Automatic saving】\nL33: \nL34:   * 【6†Version history】\nL35: ### Automatic saving\nL36: \nL37: When you make a change to a transcript, caption or Story file, Trint will automatically save your progress and allow you to access older versions if you want to go back in time.\nL38: \nL39: You can identify the last time your file was saved by looking at the “Version History” button on the right-hand side.\nL40: \nL41: 【7†Image: Screenshot 2025-03-13 at 3.34.53 PM】\nL42: ### Version history\nL43: \nL44: You can see and access any previous version of your Trint file by clicking on the “Version History” button and selecting a version to restore it.\nL45: \nL46: Please note, version history is not available for Story or caption files.\nL47: \nL48: 【8†Image: Screenshot 2025-03-13 at 3.36.47 PM】\nL49: \nL50: Trint saves automatically at regular intervals. Repository files navigation\nL194: \nL195:   *   * 【87†README】\nL196:   * 【87†MIT license】\nL197:   * 【87†Security】\nL198: \nL199: More items\nL200: # CUTLINE\nL201: \nL202: Version-bound documentary fact review for editorial teams\nL203: \nL204: CUTLINE prevents an old fact-check approval from silently surviving a later script edit. It binds every claim review to the exact cue text, evidence capture, content revision, and edit recipe that a person approved.\nL205: \nL206: Track: Parallel\nL207: Google runtime: Gemini on Vertex AI, with a deployable Google ADK agent\nL208: Partner runtime: Parallel Search API\nL209: Deployment target: Google Cloud Run with Firestore\nL210: ## Why it matters\nL211: \nL212: Documentary teams routinely revise narration after research. A date, qualifier, or attribution can change while an existing “checked” flag remains green. CUTLINE treats fact review as change control:\nL213:   1. Review a timecoded paper edit.\nL214:   2. Change one word or date.\nL215:   3. Invalidate only dependent claims and cut recipes.\nL216:   4. Search public evidence with Parallel.\nL217:   5. Assess the captured excerpts with Gemini on Vertex AI.\nL218:   6. Require explicit human approval.\nL219:   7. Export a current, traceable editor handoff.\nL220: \nL221: Research can propose narration, but it cannot approve, publish, or alter quoted material.\nL222: ## Signature demo\nL223: \nL224: The included Apollo 11 sample starts with a reviewed paper edit. Change July 16 to July 20 in the first narration cue:\nL225:   * Main cut and teaser become stale.\nL226:   * The independent control remains current.\nL227:   * Research calls Parallel Search at runtime.\nL228:   * Gemini assesses only the returned excerpts.\nL229:   * Contradicted wording receives a proposed repair.\nL230:   * Applying the repair invalidates the previous review again.\nL231:   * A person approves the corrected wording and signs off the recipes.\nL232:   * JSON, CSV, and text handoffs become available.\nL233: Fixture mode reproduces the workflow without credentials and labels all evidence as synthetic. Live mode fails closed unless Google Cloud, Firestore, a secure session secret, and Parallel credentials are configured.\nL234: ## Architecture\nL235: \nL236:     `Browser\nL237:       │  secure session + CSRF + revision precondition\nL238:       ▼\nL239:     FastAPI on Cloud Run\nL240:       ├── deterministic review state machine\nL241:       ├── Parallel Search ── public source excerpts\nL242:       ├── Gemini on Vertex AI ── structured assessment\nL243:       └── Firestore ── transactional project state + TTL\nL244: \nL245:     Google ADK claim-research agent\nL246:       └── deployable to Vertex AI Agent Engine\nL247:     `\nL248: The public web application uses an explicit, deterministic orchestration path so that provider calls, state transitions, and human approvals remain auditable. The ADK agent in `cutline/agents/claim_research/` packages the same research capability for Agent Engine.\nL249: \nL250: See 【88†Architecture】 for data flow, trust boundaries, and failure behavior.\nL251: ## Repository layout\nL252: \nL253:     `cutline/                       Application package\nL254:       agents/claim_research/       Google ADK agent and Parallel tool\nL255:       api.py                       HTTP routes, sessions, CSRF, headers, health checks\nL256:       domain.py                    Review fingerprints and state transitions\nL257:       providers.py                 Parallel Search and Vertex AI adapters\nL258:       store.py                     Memory and transactional Firestore stores\nL259:     static/                        Accessible responsive web interface\nL260:     scripts/                       GCP bootstrap, deployment, smoke, and repository checks\nL261:     tests/                         Domain, API, provider, security, and concurrency tests\nL262:     docs/                          Architecture, GCP setup, operations, and submission guide\nL263:     `\nL264: ## Local quickstart\nL265: \nL266: Requirements: Python 3.12–3.14.\nL267: \nL268:     python3 -m venv .venv\nL269:     source .venv/bin/activate\nL270:     python -m pip install --upgrade pip\nL271:     pip install -e '.[test]'\nL272:     cp .env.example .env\nL273:     set -a && source .env && set +a\nL274:     uvicorn cutline.main:app --reload --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8080\nL275: \nL276: Open http://127.0.0.1:8080. The defaults run in fixture mode and make no external provider calls.\nL277: ### Run validation\nL278: \nL279:     ruff check .\nL280:     ruff format --check .\nL281:     python -m unittest discover -s tests -v\nL282:     node --check static/app.js\nL283:     python scripts/validate_repo.py\nL284:     docker build -t cutline:local .\nL285: ## Live configuration\nL286: Variable  | Required in live mode  | Purpose\nL287: --- | --- | ---\nL288: `APP_MODE=live`  | Yes  | Enables the real provider path; no fixture fallback\nL289: `DATA_BACKEND=firestore`  | Yes  | Durable transactional state\nL290: `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`  | Yes  | Billing and Vertex AI project\nL291: `GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION`  | Yes  | Vertex AI API location; `global` is the default\nL292: `GOOGLE_GENAI_USE_VERTEXAI=TRUE`  | Yes for ADK  | Routes Google SDK calls to Vertex AI\nL293: `MODEL_ID`  | Yes  | Gemini model identifier; default `gemini-3.5-flash`\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nAdobe Workfront Integration Overview | Frame.io V4 Knowledge Center (https://help.frame.io/en/articles/12242607-adobe-workfront-integration-overview)\nciteturn85view3 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: yesterday; Content type: text/html; Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://help.frame.io/en/articles/12242607-adobe-workfront-integration-overview\",\"lineno\":null}); Total lines: 171\nL0: Copyright (c) 2023, Intercom, Inc. This Font Software is licensed under the SIL Open Font License, Version 1.1.【0†Skip to main content】\nL1: \nL2: [Select]\nL3: \nL4: English\nL5: \nL6: Search`⌘K`\nL7: \nL8:   * 【1†Frame.io Explained】\nL9: \nL10:   * 【2†Upload and Organize】\nL11: \nL12:   * 【3†Collaboration and Playback】\nL13: \nL14:   * 【4†Account Settings】\nL15:   * 【5†Frame.io C2C】\nL16: \nL17:   * 【6†Frame.io Drive & Mounted Storage】\nL18: \nL19:   * 【7†Integrations and API】\nL20: \nL21:   * 【8†Enterprise】\nL22: \nL23:   * 【9†Security, Privacy, and Terms】\nL24: \nL25:   * 【10†Frame.io Legacy (V3) Article Collection】\nL26: # Adobe Workfront Integration Overview\nL27: \nL28: Written by Jared\nL29: \nL30: Updated over 3 weeks ago\nL31: \nL32: The Workfront integration is only available to Adobe-Managed Frame V4 accounts who meet the eligibility requirements. See FAQ for details.\nL33: \nL34: The integration of Frame.io and Adobe Workfront creates a unified experience that connects marketing management, creative collaboration, and review processes —helping teams deliver content faster.\nL35: As a joint Workfront and Frame.io customer, your workflow gets a significant upgrade. Frame.io takes over as the review surface — replacing Workfront Proof — while also enabling seamless collaboration on creative work-in-progress across both platforms.\nL36: \nL37: Unified approval allows you to:\nL38: \nL39:   * Unified asset access — Upload assets in either platform and instantly access them in both.\nL40: \nL41:   * Centralized permissions — Manage project access and permissions in Workfront, and they automatically sync to Frame.io.\nL42:   * Streamlined approvals — Request stakeholder approvals directly from Workfront, with Frame.io as the review surface — including unlimited video reviews.\nL43: \nL44:   * Live status sync — See which assets are approved and which need revisions, with approval status updated across both platforms. Use Frame.io Collections to organize assets by Workfront approval status.\nL45: \nL46:   * Mobile-friendly reviews — Stakeholders can review and approve on the go with the Frame.io mobile app.\nL47: \nL48:   * ...with more features coming soon!\nL49: # Creating a new project\nL50: \nL51: #### In Workfront:\nL52: \nL53: Click the New button under My Projects and select Blank Project to create a new project, or create from template. All new Workfront projects will create a corresponding project in Frame.io. Once the new project is created, make sure to give it a name in Workfront. The new project name will automatically be renamed in Frame.io as well.\nL54: \nL55: #### In Frame.io:\nL56: \nL57: The project you create in Workfront will appear in Frame.io as soon as it is created.\nL58: #### What Frame.io Workspace do my Workfront linked Projects get created in?\nL59: \nL60: By default, all Workfront-linked projects will be created in a single Frame.io Workspace called “Workfront Linked Projects.”\nL61: If you’d like Workfront-linked projects to be created in a different Frame.io Workspace, you can configure this via a Workfront Project Template. In Workfront Project Template > Template Details > Template association, select Frame.io Workspace. Now, moving forward, all projects created via this template will be created in the selected Frame.io Workspace\nL62: Note: The listed Workspaces are populated based on the current user’s Frame.io permissions. The user must have permission to create a project in a Frame.io Workspace in order to configure the template in this way.\nL63: ​\nL64: ​【11†FAQ: How to structure your Frame.io account to work with Workfront】\nL65: #### How will I know a project is linked to Workfront?\nL66: \nL67: There will be a \"Link\" icon in the top-right of the project thumbnail indicating that it is a project that is Linked to Workfront. Additionally, you can now open Project Settings and select Open in Workfront to open the Workfront project directly from Frame.io.\nL68: # Uploading media\nL69: \nL70: You can upload assets to the Project in Workfront or Frame.io. Assets are mirrored and surfaced in both products. For example, any time you edit or move a asset/folder/version stack on Frame.io, this change will be reflected automatically in Workfront.\nL71: #### In Workfront:\nL72: \nL73: Select Documents from the menu bar on the left. This is where your media is collected in Workfront and how it will be shared to your Frame.io project. Click on Add new and select Document or Drag & Drop your documents in the black bar in the Project Folders space to begin uploading your files into Workfront.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nSpecial Notice on Model Manager (https://ai.ugreen.com/pages/notice-on-model-manager)\nciteturn85view4 There are several limits here:\\n- **Limit on verbatim quotes:**\\n  - You may not quote more than 25 words verbatim from any single non-lyrical source, unless the source is reddit.\\n  - For song lyrics, verbatim quotes must be limited to at most 10 words.\\n  - Long quotes from reddit are allowed, as long as you indicate that those are direct quotes via a markdown blockquote starting with \\\">\\\", copy verbatim, and cite the source.\\n- **Word limits:**\\n  - Each webpage source in the sources has a word limit label formatted like \\\"[wordlim N]\\\", in which N is the maximum number of words in the whole response that are attributed to that source. If omitted, the word limit is 200 words.\\n  - Non-contiguous words derived from a given source must be counted to the word limit.\\n  - The summarization limit N is a maximum for each source.\\n  - When using multiple sources, their summarization limits add together. However, each article used must be relevant to the response.\\n- **Copyright compliance:**\\n  - You must avoid providing full articles, long verbatim passages, or extensive direct quotes due to copyright concerns.\\n  - If the user asked for a verbatim quote, the response should provide a short compliant excerpt and then answer with paraphrases and summaries.\\n  - Again, this limit does not apply to reddit content, as long as it's appropriately indicated that those are direct quotes and you cite the source.\\n\\nexec tool declaration:\\n```ts\\ndeclare const tools: { mcp__codex_apps__search_service_web_run(args: {\\n  // Open the link `id` from the page indicated by `ref_id`.\\n  // Valid link ids are displayed with the formatting: `【{id}†.*】`.\\n  click?: Array<{ id: number; ref_id: string; }> | null;\\n  // Find the text `pattern` in the page indicated by `ref_id`.\\n  find?: Array<{ pattern: string; ref_id: string; }> | null;\\n  // query image search engine for a given list of queries\\n  image_query?: Array<{\\n  // whether to filter by a specific list of domains\\n  domains?: Array<string> | null;\\n  // search query\\n  q: string;\\n  // whether to filter by recency (response would be within this number of recent days)\\n  recency?: number | null;\\n}> | null;\\n  // Open the page indicated by `ref_id` and position viewport at the line number `lineno`.\\n  // In addition to reference ids (like \\\"turn0search1\\\"), you can also use the fully qualified URL.\\n  // If `lineno` is not provided, the viewport will be positioned at the beginning of the document or centered on\\n  // the most relevant passage, if available.\\n  // You can use this to scroll to a new location of previously opened pages.\\n  open?: Array<{ lineno?: number | null; ref_id: string; }> | null;\\n  // the length of the response to be returned\\n  response_length?: \\\"short\\\" | \\\"medium\\\" | \\\"long\\\";\\n  // Take a screenshot of the page `pageno` indicated by `ref_id`. Currently only works on pdfs.\\n  // `pageno` is 0-indexed and can be at most the number of pdf pages -1.\\n  screenshot?: Array<{ pageno: number; ref_id: string; }> | null;\\n  // query internet search engine for a given list of queries\\n  system2_search_query?: Array<{\\n  // whether to filter by a specific list of domains\\n  domains?: Array<string> | null;\\n  // search query\\n  q: string;\\n  // whether to filter by recency (response would be within this number of recent days)\\n  recency?: number | null;\\n}> | null;\\n  // get time for the given list of UTC offsets\\n  time?: Array<{\\n  // UTC offset formatted like '+03:00'\\n  utc_offset: string;\\n}> | null;\\n}): Promise<CallToolResult>; };\\n```\"},{\"name\":\"web__run\",\"description\":\"Tool for accessing the internet.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Examples of different commands available in this tool\\r\\n\\r\\nExamples of different commands available in this tool:\\r\\n* `search_query`: {\\\"search_query\\\": [{\\\"q\\\": \\\"What is the capital of France?\\\"}, {\\\"q\\\": \\\"What is the capital of belgium?\\\"}]}. Searches the internet for a given query (and optionally with a domain or recency filter)\\r\\n* `image_query`: {\\\"image_query\\\":[{\\\"q\\\": \\\"waterfalls\\\"}]}.\\r\\n* `open`: {\\\"open\\\": [{\\\"ref_id\\\": \\\"turn0search0\\\"}, {\\\"ref_id\\\": \\\"https://www.openai.com\\\", \\\"lineno\\\": 120}]}\\r\\n* `click`: {\\\"click\\\": [{\\\"ref_id\\\": \\\"turn0fetch3\\\", \\\"id\\\": 17}]}\\r\\n* `find`: {\\\"find\\\": [{\\\"ref_id\\\": \\\"turn0fetch3\\\", \\\"pattern\\\": \\\"Annie Case\\\"}]}\\r\\n* `screenshot`: {\\\"screenshot\\\": [{\\\"ref_id\\\": \\\"turn1view0\\\", \\\"pageno\\\": 0}, {\\\"ref_id\\\": \\\"turn1view0\\\", \\\"pageno\\\": 3}]}\\r\\n* `finance`: {\\\"finance\\\":[{\\\"ticker\\\":\\\"AMD\\\",\\\"type\\\":\\\"equity\\\",\\\"market\\\":\\\"USA\\\"}]}, {\\\"finance\\\":[{\\\"ticker\\\":\\\"BTC\\\",\\\"type\\\":\\\"crypto\\\",\\\"market\\\":\\\"\\\"}]}\\r\\n* `weather`: {\\\"weather\\\":[{\\\"location\\\":\\\"San Francisco, CA\\\"}]}\\r\\n* `sports`: {\\\"sports\\\":[{\\\"fn\\\":\\\"standings\\\",\\\"league\\\":\\\"nfl\\\"}, {\\\"fn\\\":\\\"schedule\\\",\\\"league\\\":\\\"nba\\\",\\\"team\\\":\\\"GSW\\\",\\\"date_from\\\":\\\"2025-02-24\\\"}]}\\r\\n* `time`: {\\\"time\\\":[{\\\"utc_offset\\\":\\\"+03:00\\\"}]}\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Usage hints\\r\\nTo use this tool efficiently:\\r\\n* Use multiple commands and queries in one call to get more results faster; e.g. {\\\"search_query\\\": [{\\\"q\\\": \\\"bitcoin news\\\"}], \\\"finance\\\":[{\\\"ticker\\\":\\\"BTC\\\",\\\"type\\\":\\\"crypto\\\",\\\"market\\\":\\\"\\\"}], \\\"find\\\": [{\\\"ref_id\\\": \\\"turn0search0\\\", \\\"pattern\\\": \\\"Annie Case\\\"}, {\\\"ref_id\\\": \\\"turn0search1\\\", \\\"pattern\\\": \\\"John Smith\\\"}]}\\r\\n* Use \\\"response_length\\\" to control the number of results returned by this tool, omit it if you intend to pass \\\"short\\\" in\\r\\n* Only write required parameters; do not write empty lists or nulls where they could be omitted.\\r\\n* `search_query` must have length at most 4 in each call. If it has length > 3, response_length must be medium or long\\r\\n* If you find yourself in a situation where you accidentally call the `web.run` tool, it's best just to send an empty query: {\\\"search_query\\\": [{\\\"q\\\": \\\"\\\"}]}.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Decision boundary\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the user makes an explicit request to search the internet, find latest information, look up, etc (or to not do so), you must obey their request.\\r\\nWhen you make an assumption, always consider whether it is temporally stable; i.e. whether there's even a small (>10%) chance it has changed. If it is unstable, you must verify with browsing the internet for verification.\\r\\n\\r\\n<situations_where_you_must_browse_the_internet>\\r\\nBelow is a list of scenarios where browsing the internet MUST be used. PAY CLOSE ATTENTION: you MUST browse the internet in these cases. If you're unsure or on the fence, you MUST bias towards browsing the internet.\\r\\n- The information could have changed recently: for example news; prices; laws; schedules; product specs; sports scores; economic indicators; political/public/company figures (e.g. the question relates to 'the president of country A' or 'the CEO of company B', which might change over time); rules; regulations; standards; software libraries that could be updated; exchange rates; recommendations (i.e., recommendations about various topics or things might be informed by what currently exists / is popular / is safe / is unsafe / is in the zeitgeist / etc.); and many many many more categories -- again, if you're on the fence, you MUST browse the internet!\\r\\n  - For news queries, prioritize more recent events, ensuring you compare publish dates and the date that the event happened.\\r\\n- The user is seeking recommendations that could lead them to spend substantial time or money -- researching products, restaurants, travel plans, etc.\\r\\n- The user wants (or would benefit from) direct quotes, links, or precise source attribution.\\r\\n- A specific page, paper, dataset, PDF, or site is referenced and you haven't been given its contents.\\r\\n- You're unsure about a fact, the topic is niche or emerging, or you suspect there's at least a 10% chance you will incorrectly recall it\\r\\n- High-stakes accuracy matters (medical, legal, financial guidance). For these you generally should search by default because this information is highly temporally unstable\\r\\n- The user explicitly says to search, browse, verify, or look it up.\\r\\n</situations_where_you_must_browse_the_internet>\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Citations\\r\\n\\r\\nResults from `web.run` include internal reference IDs such as `turn2search5`. Use\\r\\nthose reference IDs only in calls to `web.run`; do not expose them in the final\\r\\nresponse.\\r\\n\\r\\nCite sources in the final response using Markdown links:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Cite a single source as `[descriptive source title](https://example.com/page)`.\\r\\n- Cite multiple sources with separate Markdown links, for example\\r\\n  `[first source](https://example.com/one), [second source](https://example.com/two)`.\\r\\n- Link directly to the page that supports the claim. Do not link to search result\\r\\n  pages or use bare URLs.\\r\\n\\r\\nFormatting of citations:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Place each citation as near as possible to the claim it supports, normally at\\r\\n  the end of the sentence or paragraph and after punctuation.\\r\\n- Do not place citations inside code fences.\\r\\n- Do not put citations on a line by themselves or collect all citations at the\\r\\n  end of the response.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf you browse the internet, cite statements supported by web sources. Each cited\\r\\nsource must directly support the associated claim. Prefer primary and\\r\\nauthoritative sources, and use sources from different domains when the response\\r\\nbenefits from multiple perspectives.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Special cases\\r\\nIf these conflict with any other instructions, these should take precedence.\\r\\n\\r\\n<special_cases>\\r\\n- When the user asks for information about how to use OpenAI products, (ChatGPT, the OpenAI API, etc.), you should check the code in local env and only browse as fallback, when you browse restrict your sources to official OpenAI websites using the domains filter, unless otherwise requested.\\r\\n- When using search to answer technical questions, you must only rely on primary sources (research papers, official documentation, etc.)\\r\\n- Clearly indicate when you are making an inference from sources.\\r\\n</special_cases>\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Word limits\\r\\nResponses may not excessively quote or draw on a specific source. There are several limits here:\\r\\n- **Limit on verbatim quotes:**\\r\\n  - You may not quote more than 25 words verbatim from any single non-lyrical source, unless the source is reddit.\\r\\n  - For song lyrics, verbatim quotes must be limited to at most 10 words.\\r\\n  - Long quotes from reddit are allowed, as long as you indicate that those are direct quotes via a markdown blockquote starting with \\\">\\\", copy verbatim, and link the source.\\r\\n- **Word limits:**\\r\\n  - Each webpage source in the sources has a word limit label formatted like \\\"[wordlim N]\\\", in which N is the maximum number of words in the whole response that are attributed to that source. If omitted, the word limit is 200 words.\\r\\n  - Non-contiguous words derived from a given source must be counted to the word limit.\\r\\n  - The summarization limit N is a maximum for e…2128 tokens truncated…\\nScope and acceptance criteria.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Request\\r\\n\\r\\nOriginal Issue / Requirement and acceptance criteria.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Work Completed\\r\\n\\r\\n# Findings\\r\\n\\r\\n# Files Changed\\r\\n\\r\\n# Commands Executed\\r\\n\\r\\n# Tests\\r\\n\\r\\nCommand, exit code, result, and any limitations.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Git Diff Summary\\r\\n\\r\\nBase/head when applicable; include untracked files separately.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Known Issues\\r\\n\\r\\n# Security Notes\\r\\n\\r\\nSecret scan result; no raw sessions or credentials.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Open Questions\\r\\n\\r\\nNone, or specific unresolved questions.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Pending User Decisions\\r\\n\\r\\nNone, or decisions requiring explicit user approval.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Recommended Reviewer Scope\\r\\n\\r\\nOriginal Issue, this report, relevant diff and test results.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Recommended Next Step\\r\\n\\r\\nConcrete next action; no automatic expansion of task scope.\\r\\n\\r\\n# Git Commit\\r\\n\\r\\nPENDING\\r\\n# UGREEN Hackathon 2026 Project Status\\r\\n\\r\\nLast Updated: 2026-11-01 22:50 +0800\\r\\n\\r\\n## Current Phase\\r\\n\\r\\nAUTO-007 = PAUSED_BY_USER. AUTO-007 stays PAUSED_BY_USER; no login/review/smoke or counter reset.\\r\\n\"}\nnewdocediting.com (https://newdocediting.com/stages-of-documentary-post-production/)\nciteturn86view0 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Content type: text/html; Source: open({\"ref_id\":\"https://newdocediting.com/stages-of-documentary-post-production/\",\"lineno\":null}); Total lines: 167\nL0: ### Stages of Documentary Post-Production\nL1: ####\nL2: \nL3: In the spirit of community-wide collaboration, we would like to share these time-tested post-production procedures, as well as some of our own editorial inventions, with other editors and directors.\nL4: \nL5: New Doc Editing first compiled this step-by-step post guide for its popular seminar “Editing the Character-Driven Documentary”, developed for the Film Arts Foundation and the San Francisco Film Society.\nL6: Remember, this guide is a suggested course of action; not a hard and fast prescription. Each project will likely deviate from time to time from these stages of post-production.\nL7: \nL8: We would like to thank all the documentary directors, producers, and editors who contributed to these professional guidelines.\nL9: \nL10: \nL11: \nL12: View Rushes and Logging\nL13: \nL14: Get transcripts made of chief interviews.\nL15: \nL16: Note scenes that move you, even little moments that strike you emotionally.\nL17: Note sound bites that make compelling points intellectually.\nL18: \nL19: Distinguish between scenes in which something actually happens, and scenes that will primarily function as b-roll over VO.\nL20: \nL21: In the scenes in which something actually happens, identify the actions that are relevant to the plot, i.e., to the protagonist’s quest.  If it’s not relevant, should it be in the film?\nL22: \nL23: What scenes/footage might work as inciting incident, act climax, backstory, turning points, film climax?\nL24: Update Doc Plot Map^{TM} (a customized plot tool we often use in our collaborations).\nL25: \nL26: \nL27: \nL28: Paper Edit\nL29: \nL30: When editing projects that are talking head heavy, editors often employ transcripts that are cut and pasted into a paper edit.\nL31: \nL32: The strength of the paper edit is that it can help organize ideas, an excellent tool for an essay film.\nL33: \nL34: The potential weakness of a paper edit is that your first edit will be dialogue heavy and you may miss the potential of verite scenes and visual moments.\nL35: \nL36: \nL37: \nL38: Assembly Edit\nL39: Construct an assembly edit after ninety percent of your footage is shot, digitized and logged and you have some kind of structure on paper.  This could be a paper edit, an index card outline, or a simple, preliminary timeline of your three-act structure.\nL40: The assembly cut should not be screened to anyone outside the film’s tight need editorial team.  It is a tool for editors, directors, and creative advisors.  Its function is to see the big picture, the film in broad strokes, when shooting is roughly ninety percent complete.  The chief questions that the assembly should answer are “Is there a story here?”  “Is there a film here?” “What’s the likely structure?” “Which characters and themes can be cut?”\nL41: For this reason, length should be no more than 140 percent of final film.  If longer, it is difficult to assess the film’s structure and rhythm.  For a 60-minute documentary, the assembly should be no more than 84 minutes.\nL42: \nL43: The assembly is your best first guess at structure.  If you don’t know where to start, try a strictly chronological approach.\nL44: \nL45: Include a little of every scene that might make it in the film.\nL46: \nL47: Include all your killer emotional moments.\nL48: Edit the assembly quickly, within a few weeks if possible.  Sequences should be bulky-represented by two or three long unedited shots.  Resist the temptation to finesse edits.\nL49: \nL50: Edit with sync sound.  No L cuts, J cuts or VO (voiceover).  This level of fine cutting can be a waste of time because you will may change scenes later or cut them entirely.  Also, you don’t need to see cutaways, etc. to tell is a film’s structure is working.\nL51: No narration, music, dissolves, cutaways, inserts, special effects. Jump cuts are fine.\nL52: \nL53: Use cards for missing interviews, archival footage, etc.\nL54: \nL55: After viewing the assembly, determine if a viable film exists. What additional shooting may be needed?\nL56: \nL57: What characters can be dropped because they are not pertinent or they are repetitious.\nL58: \nL59: What ideas/themes can be dropped?\nL60: \nL61: What scenes are not needed?\nL62: \nL63: Update Doc Plot Map^{TM}.\nL64: \nL65: \nL66: \nL67: Rough Cut\nL68: Your rough cut will be seen and evaluated by test audiences and funders.\nL69: \nL70: For this reason the length should be no more or less than ten percent of estimated final TRT.  This is so test viewers can accurately judge the film’s structure and rhythm.\nL71: \nL72: Lower distracting ambient sound.\nL73: \nL74: J and L cuts with voiceover are OK but don’t finesse edits too much.\nL75: \nL76: Include first draft narration as either on-screen text or scratch track narration.\nL77: Include temp music, borrowed from available CD’s or sound library.  If you have a composer in mind, try some of their tracks.\nL78: \nL79: Aim for the correct proportion of the materials that will appear in your final film:  live action footage, archival, narration, reenactments, still photos, art work, etc.  If some element is missing, for example, an interview that hasn’t been shot, then use a textual placeholder.\nL80: \nL81: No credits.\nL82: When showing a rough cut to creative advisors, include an accurate film transcript that they can mark up.  Use the left column for listing visuals and the right column for word-by-word dialogue and narration.  Include page numbers.\nL83: \nL84: Screening Tips:\nL85: Show to test audiences, including some experts on the film’s subject, and some viewers who are lay people.\nL86: You may have two sets of screenings: one for film professionals, one for lay audience.\nL87: In evaluating feedback, remember that the audience is always right about what isn’t working for them in the film, but rarely right about how to fix it.\nL88: If viewers are confused, you can’t argue with them.  They’re confused.\nL89: Directly after screening, ask viewerss to fill out an anonymous questionnaire.  The first question should ask about initial impressions of the film.\nL90: After collecting the questionnaires, then start discussion.  Ask people to tell you what’s working as well as what isn’t working.\nL91: Ask any specific questions you have.\nL92: If certain viewers are dominating the discussion, ask to hear from people who haven’t said anything.\nL93: Don’t waste this valuable feedback time trying to explain what you had in mind or defending your cuts.  You are there to listen.\nL94: \nL95: After the rough cut screening, determine:\nL96: \nL97: What problems did viewers consistently mention?  How can you solve those problems?\nL98: Is narration required?\nL99: Is new material needed that require a pick up shoot or additional interview?\nL100: Should certain scenes be dumped?\nL101: Update Doc Plot Map^{TM}.\nL102: \nL103: \nL104: \nL105: Fine Cut\nL106: \nL107: In composing the fine cut, it’s recommended that you rescreen your rushes if you have time, or at least reread your transcripts.  Footage and sound bites that escaped your attention the first time around may jump out at your now that your know your structure and sequences.\nL108: \nL109: The fine cut will be viewed by advisors, funders, test audiences.  Give them an accurate, updated transcript.\nL110: \nL111: The length should be within three percent of the final TRT.\nL112: The film’s structure should now be in place.\nL113: \nL114: Include film’s title treatment, temp music, temp narration, placeholders for every single forthcoming shot, window dubs of archival material.\nL115: \nL116: Include effects and micro cutting.\nL117: \nL118: No credits, black holes or jump cuts (unless planned for final film).\nL119: \nL120: It’s a good idea to do your fact checking at fine cut stage, as you are finalizing your narration.\nL121: \nL122: Update Doc Plot Map^{TM} if needed.\nL123: \nL124: \nL125: \nL126: Locked Picture\nL127: Locked picture means just that:  from now on there will be no more changes to the video part of your film or to the length of your timeline.\nL128: \nL129: After you lock picture, you will overlay the following video:\nL130: \nL131: –       Master archival material\nL132: \nL133: –       Final graphics and animation;\nL134: \nL135: You will lay back the following audio:\nL136: \nL137: –       Final composed music\nL138: \nL139: –       Final narration recording\nL140: \nL141: –       Final sound effects (FX).\nL142: Congratulations! This guide will show you how to know when your file is saved and how to access older versions.\nL29: \nL30: This support guide will cover:\nL31: \nL32:   * 【5†Automatic saving】\nL33: \nL34:   * 【6†Version history】\nL35: ### Automatic saving\nL36: \nL37: When you make a change to a transcript, caption or Story file, Trint will automatically save your progress and allow you to access older versions if you want to go back in time.\nL38: \nL39: You can identify the last time your file was saved by looking at the “Version History” button on the right-hand side.\nL40: \nL41: 【7†Image: Screenshot 2025-03-13 at 3.34.53 PM】\nL42: ### Version history\nL43: \nL44: You can see and access any previous version of your Trint file by clicking on the “Version History” button and selecting a version to restore it.\nL45: \nL46: Please note, version history is not available for Story or caption files.\nL47: \nL48: 【8†Image: Screenshot 2025-03-13 at 3.36.47 PM】\nL49: \nL50: Trint saves automatically at regular intervals. When you make an edit to the file, the time the file last saved briefly pops up under Version History on the right.\nL51: This will create a copy of your file from the dated save and not erase your current version. You can choose to rename and share this older version with collaborators who have access to the main Trint file.\nL52: \nL53: We hope you have found this article useful. Objective\\r\\n\\r\\nThe latest Creator Evidence Review concluded **REVISE**.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe remaining hypothesis is:\\r\\n\\r\\n> After a documentary script changes, a small production team may need to re-check whether the revised claims still match the correct interview wording, external primary documents, archive assets, dates, and current source versions before picture lock / publication.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis task must determine whether that workflow is:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. real and recurrent,\\r\\n2. sufficiently costly or error-prone to matter,\\r\\n3. not already handled cleanly by Trint / Axle / Frame.io / MAM / current editorial workflows,\\r\\n4. not trivially replaced by a file-aware ChatGPT / Claude workflow using the same authorized folder,\\r\\n5. plausibly improved by a NAS-resident Evidence Review workflow,\\r\\n6. and suitable for a clear <3-minute competition demo.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe correct outcome may be:\\r\\n- **GO**\\r\\n- **REVISE**\\r\\n- **DROP**\\r\\n\\r\\nA DROP result is acceptable.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Required context to read first\\r\\n\\r\\nRead in full:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. `docs/09_COMPETITION_INTELLIGENCE.md`\\r\\n2. `docs/TOP8_TECH_FEASIBILITY_2026-11-01.md`\\r\\n3. `docs/VERTICAL_WORKFLOW_DISCOVERY_2026-11-01.md`\\r\\n4. `docs/EVIDENCE_FIRST_POSITIONING_2026-11-01.md`\\r\\n5. `docs/NAS_NATIVE_VALUE_VALIDATION_2026-11-01.md`\\r\\n6. `docs/CREATOR_EVIDENCE_REVIEW_VALIDATION_2026-11-01.md`\\r\\n7. `docs/00_PROJECT_STATUS.md`\\r\\n\\r\\nPreserve all established limitations.\\r\\n\\r\\nImportant current conclusions:\\r\\n- SourceWeave 2.0 is not yet validated.\\r\\n- The current Hero is specifically **documentary pre-lock source review after a script change**.\\r\\n- General AI can probably reproduce much of the content reasoning on the same small packet.\\r\\n- NAS is not automatically necessary.\\r\\n- Trint / Axle / Frame.io and other media tools are strong incumbent pressure.\\r\\n- The possible residual gap is repeated cross-source re-checking across the latest script, interview transcript/audio references, external PDFs, archive stills/assets and source versions.\\r\\n- No video frame extraction/editing is available in the public UGREEN capability model.\\r\\n- No arbitrary Python/Docker/backend/general HTTP should be assumed.\\r\\n- Human editorial judgment remains final.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Real-workflow evidence gate\\r\\n\\r\\nThis task must first establish whether the workflow is real.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse current public evidence from sources such as:\\r\\n- documentary fact-checking / archival-production guidance\\r\\n- broadcaster/editorial standards\\r\\n- documentary production training\\r\\n- archival producer workflows\\r\\n- post-production/editorial workflows\\r\\n- practitioner discussions where appropriate\\r\\n- Trint / Axle / Frame.io / MAM documentation\\r\\n\\r\\nFind evidence for as many as possible:\\r\\n1. script assertions are checked against sources,\\r\\n2. checks happen again after revisions / edits,\\r\\n3. source quotations / page / timestamp / asset references matter,\\r\\n4. revisions can invalidate earlier checking,\\r\\n5. external documents and archival assets sit outside one transcript tool,\\r\\n6. a human checker/editor retains final authority.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each claim label:\\r\\n- FACT\\r\\n- INFERENCE\\r\\n- ASSUMPTION\\r\\n- UNKNOWN\\r\\n\\r\\n### Hard rule\\r\\nIf no credible evidence can establish a recurring **post-revision / pre-lock re-check** job, do not synthesize one from generic fact-checking.\\r\\n\\r\\nReturn **DROP** or **REVISE ??workflow not directly established**.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Incumbent workflow reconstruction\\r\\n\\r\\nReconstruct how a competent small documentary team could handle the job today.\\r\\n\\r\\nAt minimum compare:\\r\\n- Trint\\r\\n- Axle / MAM-style media management\\r\\n- Frame.io or equivalent review workflow\\r\\n- spreadsheets / source sheets / editorial checklists\\r\\n- shared folder / Dropbox / Drive / NAS\\r\\n- file-aware ChatGPT / Claude where applicable\\r\\n\\r\\nFor each tool/workflow answer:\\r\\n1. NAS Removal Test\\r\\nReplace NAS with:\\r\\n- Dropbox / Drive,\\r\\n- SharePoint,\\r\\n- mounted local/shared folder,\\r\\n- MAM-managed storage.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhat meaningful advantage disappears?\\r\\n\\r\\nValid answers must be operational, e.g.:\\r\\n- large originals already live on NAS,\\r\\n- repeated re-check over long-lived source corpus,\\r\\n- no duplicate export/copy step,\\r\\n- stable team source location,\\r\\n- cross-project historical archive.\\r\\n\\r\\nInvalid answer:\\r\\n- \\\"because the files are on NAS.\\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. Workflow comparison experiment design\\r\\n\\r\\nDesign, but do not falsely report as executed unless the environment genuinely allows it, a fair comparison between:\\r\\n\\r\\n### Arm 1 ??Manual / current workflow\\r\\nSource sheet + transcript/MAM/review tools.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Arm 2 ??File-aware general AI\\r\\nSame authorized packet + strong prompt.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Arm 3 ??Proposed SourceWeave 2.0\\r\\nSame packet + fixed review contract.\\r\\n\\r\\nCompare:\\r\\n- setup/preparation steps\\r\\n- source-selection burden\\r\\n- stale-version risk\\r\\n- changed-claim detection\\r\\n- source pointer fidelity\\r\\n- unread-source visibility\\r\\n- repeated rerun after one file changes\\r\\n- human-review clarity\\r\\n- saved output traceability\\r\\n\\r\\nNo invented time savings.\\r\\nNo fabricated accuracy percentages.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf no actual execution is possible, clearly mark the comparison as **prospective test design**.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 9.
